# Fight for Peace — Features and Decisions

This is the living product and game-design decision log for *Fight for Peace*. It records confirmed requirements separately from proposals and unresolved questions. Detailed implementation planning will follow after the Combat Lab design is approved.

## Status labels

- **Confirmed** — explicitly selected as part of the game design.
- **Proposed** — recommended for discussion; not yet a requirement.
- **Deferred** — intended for a later milestone rather than the first Combat Lab release.
- **Planned** — approved as a future refinement module whose detailed design is not yet decided.

## 1. Game vision

- **Confirmed:** *Fight for Peace* is a browser-based 2D side-view action game inspired by classic arcade brawlers such as *Captain Commando*, *Knights of the Round*, and *Final Fight*.
- **Confirmed:** Its defining feature is a brawler structure with deeper fighting-game mechanics.
- **Confirmed:** The protagonists value peace and fight to protect their village, loved ones, and relatives from specific threats.
- **Confirmed:** Each stage has its own story, reason for fighting, environment, enemies, boss, items, weapons, attack patterns, and ending.
- **Confirmed:** The first content milestone is Stage 1. Later stages extend the same combat engine through data-driven content.
- **Confirmed:** The combat engine is a permanent **Combat Constitution** for the complete game and its downloadable content. Future stages and canon-compatible side stories may add data-defined stages, heroes, moves, weapons, mounts, environments, enemies, bosses, encounters, and presentation, but may not fork, replace, or redesign the universal combat rules. Standing, aerial, low and ground combat; defense; resources; cancels; combo context; scaling; anti-infinite limits; weapons; environmental eligibility; co-op legality; deterministic resolution; and input abstraction remain shared across all content.
- **Confirmed:** Engine maintenance may fix defects or improve performance, security, browser compatibility, networking reliability, accessibility, diagnostics, and authoring tools only when regression tests prove that validated gameplay behavior and content legality remain unchanged. Content that cannot fit the shared rules must be redesigned as an authored combination of existing systems or deferred rather than creating a DLC-only combat fork.

## 2. Narrative and localization

- **Confirmed:** The village's wise elder analyzes threats, establishes goals, and motivates the heroes.
- **Confirmed:** Stage 4 is **The Weight of Inheritance / 传承之重 / Die Last des Erbes**, approximately six weeks after Stage 3. Its core identity is a lawful evidence-road defense in which Tomas's false account drives Kael to attack an authenticated evidence convoy. The four village heroes protect the convoy and civilians, defeat and capture Kael alive, and begin an evidence review that dismantles his inherited grievance without prematurely revealing his later neutrality. The title refers to inherited resentment, manipulated history, responsibility, and personal choice rather than blood alone.
- **Confirmed:** Stage 4 uses a **Five-Act Chain-of-Custody Route** through Council Transfer Yard, Changling Trade Road, Damaged Waystation, Broken-Road Pursuit, and Evidence-Facility Approach. The convoy advances through authored transitions between secure anchors rather than persistent escort AI. The heroes defend travelers, open the mandatory waystation evacuation corridor, may optionally rescue an annex group and recover a separated recorder, observe Soren and Chen Yue's growing doubt, and finally defeat and capture Kael after he protects their withdrawal and nearby civilians. The structure keeps evidence central, prevents vehicle babysitting, and supports every valid solo or unique co-op selection.
- **Confirmed:** Stage 4 uses a **Three-Part Chain-of-Custody Ledger**. Sealed Originals are Secure, Disturbed, or Recovered and cannot be permanently destroyed; any breach produces an authored recovery and rank consequence. The neutral archivist and civilians move through Protected, Evacuating, and Cleared states without ordinary Health or death, with at most one bounded marked disruption before safe completion. The supporting Convoy Recorder is Intact, Damaged, or Lost; its condition affects score, achievement progress, and Museum content but never gates mandatory truth because sealed originals, hashes, and working copies remain. The stage has no hidden failure timer, plot-erasing mistake, or continuous escort AI.
- **Confirmed:** Soren Beck and Chen Yue use **Resolve-Limited Rival Appearances** in Stage 4 rather than boss, lieutenant, loot, or custody roles. Soren's defensive-savate roadblock ends when he disengages to protect endangered travelers. Chen's non-injurious archivist-restraint attempt ends when she abandons it to treat waystation civilians. Their bounded Resolve exits preview their later playable identities without being farmable. At the final approach they urge withdrawal; Kael sends them to escort civilians and remains for a complete boss battle that must be genuinely won.
- **Confirmed:** Stage 4's Kael encounter is a **Three-Phase Empty-Hand Boss**. Measured Pursuit uses pressure boxing, body counters, low kicks, lane cutting, and disciplined forward movement. Chain Control adds clinches, linked takedowns, controlled ground transitions, joint control, throws, readable recovery, and co-op partner rescue. Burdened Resolve adds tighter defense, Exact Blocks, Counter conversions, and one defense-consuming Combo Breaker after his companions leave, without armour, rage immunity, or reinforcements. Kael follows all shared combat/anti-infinite rules, receives no statistics from evidence condition, and must be genuinely defeated before nonlethal restraint, assessment, and live custody.
- **Confirmed:** Stage 4's enemy family is Road Blocker, Baton Interceptor, mounted-to-dismounted Bike Pursuer, and temporary-hold Restraint Controller. Extreme adds objective-pressuring Convoy Breaker, whose marked designated actions alone may worsen convoy/support condition; Ultimate adds Counter Grappler with advanced defense and short ground conversions but not Kael's full chain complexity. Solo permits one major objective action and co-op at most two staggered actions against different targets. All follow Kael's nonlethal order and use ordinary modern road equipment without firearms, military/sponsor identity, executions, lethal crashes, or inescapable restraint.
- **Confirmed:** Stage 4's Broken-Road Pursuit uses a **Bounded Pursuit with Complete On-Foot Fallback**. Solo receives one utility bike and co-op one per hero; readable depth lanes, capped acceleration, braking, hazard dodge, one mounted strike, and voluntary dismount use inherited mount controls. All bikes have separate Stability and zero causes safe nonlethal dismount. Dismounted heroes continue through an authored roadside route and regroup at the next convoy anchor, so bike loss never fails the stage or strands a partner. Mounted performance affects pursuit score and recorder condition only. Bikes are stage-local, non-Store, non-persistent, and add no firearms, traffic simulation, civilian collision, free roaming, or explosive crash.
- **Confirmed:** Stage 4 introduces the **Folding Road Baton** and **Tire Iron** as its two pickable tools. The Baton is fast and Resolve/interrupt focused; the Tire Iron is slower, stronger against guard and tagged road objects, and cannot damage sealed evidence or protected equipment through contextual actions. Both have unique standing, aerial, low, and ground routes under shared Attack/`J` lifecycle rules and never auto-restrain or bypass Resolve. Held tools are safely stowed and restored across bike transitions while mounted play uses the universal strike. They are stage pickups only, not pre-equipped, Store, or persistent items; later reuse in *The Medicine Road* shares the same definitions.
- **Confirmed:** Stage 4 uses a **Deterministic Roadside Impact Network**: transfer-yard handcarts/cases/barriers; trade-road barriers/crates/guardrails/frames; waystation furniture/shutters/carts/shelving; warned broken-road cargo/stone/barrier hazards; and evidence-facility dividers/document carts/retractable barrier. All use shared damage/Resolve/scaling/anti-infinite rules and identical solo/co-op schedules. They cannot crush civilians, harm protected custody, destroy mandatory evidence, block the only route, open lethal falls, or create lethal crashes. Enemy/mandatory damage is not player collateral, and reduced effects preserve warnings, collision, and timing.
- **Confirmed:** Stage 4's localized **Roadside Provision Set** is Life Waystation Noodle Box / 驿站面餐盒 / Nudelbox der Raststation, Defense Citrus Electrolyte Drink / 柑橘电解质饮料 / Zitrus-Elektrolytgetränk, Attack-Bar Roasted Nut Bar / 烤坚果棒 / Gerösteter Nussriegel, and balanced Convoy Lunchbox / 护送餐盒 / Proviantbox des Geleits. Medium solo uses 3/2/2 common items, co-op adds one per common category, and each active hero receives one pre-Kael lunchbox. Easy adds one per category; Hard/Extreme/Ultimate remove 1/2/3 common items without eliminating a category or lunchbox. Safe placement, shared `J`, ownership, checkpoint, anti-duplication, and non-medical abstraction rules apply.
- **Confirmed:** Stage 4's optional branches are **No One Left at the Waystation / 驿站不留一人 / Niemand bleibt an der Raststation**, directly evacuating an annex group otherwise rescued canonically by the Council and discovering a text-free road-guardian token, and **The Record Survives / 记录留存 / Die Aufzeichnung bleibt erhalten**, recovering the recorder Intact or Damaged before a valid Lost outcome. Both in one eligible run grant **People and Proof Preserved / 人证物证皆保全 / Menschen und Beweise bewahrt** and upgrade an early road-guardian Museum exhibit that gains Wayfarer Watch commentary later. Branches never gate S, capture, mandatory truth, Stage 5, or base Museum; Cheat-Assisted play may reveal Museum content but grants no branch achievement.
- **Confirmed:** Stage 4's 100-point **Chain-of-Custody Guardian Grade** is Evidence/Chain of Custody 40, Archivist/Traveler/Civilian Safety 25, Combat/Nonlethal Control 25, and Resource/Active-Time Efficiency 10, with candidate B/A/S thresholds 60/75/90. Clear requires Kael live custody, authenticated-original delivery, and safe mandatory actors. S permits either one recovered evidence breach or one resolved civilian disruption, not both, and requires S-band collateral. Optional branches are not required; Lost recorder has no automatic cap; repeated avoidable breaches cap A and major avoidable protected damage caps B. Branch time is compensated, invalid states cannot be farmed, and Cheat-Assisted completion grants story progress but no competitive/economy/prestige result.
- **Confirmed:** Stage 4 has eight achievements: eligible clear; both branch titles; People and Proof Preserved; **Unbroken Chain / 证据链无损 / Lückenlose Beweiskette**; **Safe Testimony / 证人安然 / Sichere Aussage**; **Broken Road, Unbroken Ride / 断路疾行 / Ungebrochene Fahrt**; and optional co-op **Together on the Road / 同路并肩 / Gemeinsam auf der Straße**. Any canonical clear grants Stage 5, mandatory Chronicle/Museum records, and Store visibility for the four heroes' Road and Convoy Guardian and Traveler's Homecoming outfits; first eligible clear additionally grants Credits, first A the text-free Verified Passage emblem, and first S **Keeper of the Chain / 证据链守护者 / Hüter der Beweiskette**. Kael remains locked until the Wayfarer Watch milestone. Co-op is never required, and Cheat-Assisted completion receives only the approved progression, mandatory records, and catalogue visibility without competitive, economy, achievement, or prestige rewards.
- **Confirmed:** Stage 4's hybrid post-capture presentation is **Truth Under Seal / 封存之下的真相 / Wahrheit unter Siegel**. A short skippable opening shows Kael, Soren Beck, and Chen Yue protecting unrelated travelers before Tomas's courier delivers manipulated convoy instructions. After Kael's genuine defeat and dignified live custody, a neutral examiner connects Garran's invoice and batch, Tomas's formula-dispute and broker trail, Xu Lian's sealed records, and Tomas's communications to Kael. Sheng Guo distinguishes the father's real betrayal from the unjust collective exile; Kael accepts responsibility, suspends allegiance to Tomas, and chooses neutrality without instant absolution or village membership. Xu Lian's identity is disclosed while any meeting remains Kael's choice, and historical contradictions trigger Stage 5. Flow footage is text-free and low-motion; evidence exposition remains in-engine with English, Simplified Chinese, and German subtitles/transcripts, still fallback, skip/review support, and no branch-dependent alteration of canonical truth.
- **Confirmed:** Stage 4's **Burden of Blood** family uses seven adaptive cues from convoy departure through Kael's neutral choice, with traveling strings, wheel-like percussion, restrained modern rhythm, unresolved harmony, a clarifying Wayfarer motif, and only faint sponsor color during evidence review. Evidence/chain confirmations, civilian warnings, motorbike Stability, defensive feedback, partner rescue, and Kael's telegraphed grapple/phase sounds take mix priority through bounded music ducking. Essential audio has visual and optional vibration equivalents; categories are independently adjustable; music never controls gameplay; and generated music requires human loop editing, provenance, and commercial-use verification.
- **Confirmed:** Stage 4 uses a **Five-Anchor Evidence Snapshot** after convoy release, trade-road traveler protection, waystation clearance, pursuit resolution, and the final approach before Kael. Exact snapshots preserve heroes, resources, ownership, evidence, protected actors, branches, recorder, motorbikes, scenery, collateral, custody, score, time, eligibility, deterministic encounters, and co-op authority without permitting rerolls, repair, duplication, state improvement, or repeat rewards. Validated technical recovery and one transactional checkpoint-only suspension preserve eligibility. Voluntary or defeat-based checkpoint continuation grants canonical completion, mandatory records, and Stage 5 access but becomes a Continued Run with no competitive score, rank, Credits, performance achievement, emblem, or title.
- **Confirmed:** Stage 4's **Ten-Encounter Convoy Pressure Curve** targets 35–45 minutes first-clear and 25–30 minutes experienced replay: two mandatory ordinary encounters in each of five acts, plus bounded Soren and Chen Yue appearances, Kael, and at most one brief engagement per optional branch. Solo ordinary-enemy baselines are approximately 20/28/32/35/38 from Easy through Ultimate; Extreme adds two Convoy Breakers and Ultimate also adds two Counter Grapplers. Co-op adds 35–40% complementary ordinary pressure without duplicating authored confrontations. Simultaneous ordinary pressure caps at four solo/six co-op and approaching objective actions at one/two. Higher difficulty strengthens composition and behavior without removing warnings, changing canon, enabling off-screen objective loss, unfair simultaneous grapples, or infinite combos; exact counts remain tunable playtest baselines.
- **Confirmed:** Stage 4's localized **Protect the Chain / 守护证据链 / Schützt die Beweiskette** package keeps Guide as briefing/pause/Help reference for objective states, warnings, riding, live capture, and eligibility, while Academy supplies optional learn-by-doing **Convoy Interruption**, **Road Control**, and **Break the Hold** lessons. First clear unlocks the advanced **Kael Matchup Lab** for authentic grapple defense, recovery, Exact Block, Combo Breaker, and punish training. Lessons never gate progress, rank, achievements, techniques, or power and support EN/zh-Hans/DE, current bindings, gamepads, captions, non-colour cues, replayable demonstrations, and a solo training partner for co-op rescue practice.
- **Confirmed:** Stage 4 adds no new cheat. Shared hero codes apply; `TRUSTYTOOLS` affects carried Baton/Tire Iron integrity only, `STEADYRIDE` protects player Stability without bypassing riding, `QUIETCROWD` reduces commitment without removing authored rivals/variants/boss, `FULLPANTRY` affects only eligible ordinary recovery, and `HOMECOMING` restores the exact latest snapshot. `SAFEPASSAGE` is Not Applicable because mandatory evidence and protected actors cannot enter completion-blocking permanent loss, and it cannot preserve grading state. Cheat-Assisted clears retain Stage 5, difficulties, canon and mandatory discovery/catalogue access but no competitive score, rank, Credits, performance achievement, emblem, title, or record.
- **Confirmed:** Stage 4 uses the **Evidence-Road Accessibility Profile**. Redundant symbols/text/audio/captions/screen-reader cues, target lines, remapped prompts, independent motion/effect reductions, steering/dead-zone/camera controls, hold/toggle inputs, and non-colour co-op alerts remain Standard-eligible when timing is unchanged. **Extended Protection Window**, **Guided Road Control**, and **Extended Grapple Response** materially change timing/control and therefore create separately recorded Assisted results. Assisted play retains Story, ordinary Credits, purchases, Hero Mastery, Museum progress, and an Assisted grade, but not Standard/Open comparison or achievements explicitly testing original riding/response timing. Shared co-op aids require readiness consent; no objective requires hearing, colour, rapid tapping, pointer, camera motion, or vibration.
- **Confirmed:** Stage 4's visual-only **Road and Convoy Guardian** Store family gives Mountain Guardian **Stoneway Escort / 石道护运装 / Steinweg-Geleitschutz**, River Scout **Currentline Courier / 流线信使装 / Strömungskurier-Outfit**, Wind Sentinel **Crosswind Interceptor / 侧风截卫装 / Seitenwind-Abfangschutz**, and Fire Warden **Ember Convoy Guard / 烬火护运装 / Glut-Geleitschutz**. Each is a character-specific road-service redesign with protected riding clothing and text-free civil-protection identification, preserves silhouette/anchors/readability, supplies no mechanics or statistics, and is individually Credit-purchasable after catalogue reveal for any legal loadout.
- **Confirmed:** Stage 4's visual-only **Traveler's Homecoming** Store family gives Mountain Guardian **Homeward Stone / 归途磐石装 / Stein der Heimkehr**, River Scout **Returning Current / 归流礼装 / Heimkehrende Strömung**, Wind Sentinel **Clear-Wind Homecoming / 晴风归乡装 / Heimkehr im klaren Wind**, and Fire Warden **Hearthfire Return / 暖炉归乡装 / Heimkehr zum Herdfeuer**. The four modern heritage designs use distinct tailored clothing and restrained bridge, river, wind-line, or hearth symbolism without historical-costume, military, literal-element, statistic, or combat effects. Each preserves its hero's silhouette/anchors and is individually Credit-purchasable after reveal for any legal loadout.
- **Confirmed:** Kael's Stage 4 default is **Independent Road Fighter**: a compact athletic 24–25-year-old pressure boxer/wrestler in a fitted dark-indigo and charcoal road jacket, muted-rust/pale-sand accents, concealed rib padding, readable grappling gloves, articulated trousers, flexible road-wrestling boots, and a personal text-free three-path training token with no village/sponsor/Compact affiliation. One model supports Traveler Protector, Convoy Interceptor, fatigue-only Burdened Resolve, and treated Live Custody states without armour/rage transformation. It communicates a capable, misled, responsible opponent and later playable base without implying village membership.
- **Confirmed:** At Kael's approved Wayfarer Watch/free-mode unlock, his default is granted and two visual-only Stage 4 alternatives become individually Credit-purchasable: **Wayfarer Roadguard / 行旅守望装 / Wegwächter-Outfit**, a blue-grey/forest-green independent road-service design, and **Chosen Road Attire / 自择之路礼装 / Gewand des selbst gewählten Weges**, a deep-forest/warm-stone personal-heritage design whose joining paths symbolize self-determined identity. Neither implies village membership, predetermines Xu Lian's relationship, grants mechanics, or reveals sponsor/Compact information.
- **Confirmed:** Soren Beck and Chen Yue use an **Independent Training Companions** visual family rather than a uniform. Tall long-legged Soren wears fitted ash-blue/cream/mustard road-savate clothing; compact balanced Chen wears muted-plum/smoke-grey/sage judo-response clothing with a secured ordinary response pouch and low bun. Both carry personal three-path training tokens and use Traveler Protector, Misdirected Interceptor, and Civilian Withdrawal states, with Soren guarding evacuation and Chen treating civilians. Neither has boss/villain/sponsor/supernatural/custody presentation; the models become spoiler-safe later-playable bases without prematurely creating the Wayfarer Watch.
- **Confirmed:** Stage 4's localized **Chain-of-Custody Result Ledger** reports Evidence Integrity, People Protected, Combat and Restraint, Road Performance, and Recognition and Records, including every state, breach/recovery, attribution, grade component/cap, branch, reward, eligibility/configuration, recovery, and compatible personal-best comparison. Cheat-Assisted/Continued runs retain the narrative ledger without competitive result, while Assisted records compare separately. Automatic Highlight detection/screenshots remain Curated-Mission-only; Stage 4 stores only lightweight authored-event replay bookmarks with no real-time analysis.
- **Confirmed:** Stage 4's **Evidence-to-Archive Acceptance Matrix** covers all 4 solo heroes and 6 unique pairs; every difficulty, loadout/ruleset, input, co-op/network, browser/performance, objective-state, encounter, character, item/environment, persistence, grading/reward, ledger/bookmark, recovery/version, localization, learning, wardrobe, media/audio, and accessibility path. Release requires zero Critical/High defects plus negative proof against permanent evidence/civilian loss, hidden change, softlock, infinite/unfair combat, unreadable Kael, lethal/protected damage, restore/reroll/duplication/reward exploits, duplicate heroes or bad attribution, spoiler leakage, scripted Kael defeat, Story Highlights, and configuration-dependent simulation. Exact production values remain deferred validation.
- **Confirmed:** Stage 4's **Deterministic Objective Contract** tracks one aggregate sealed evidence case plus separate archivist, trade-road traveler, main-waystation, and optional-annex units. Each protected unit permits at most one disruption per run and only one may be active globally. Exactly two predetermined breach opportunities embed inside existing encounters; a second recovered breach caps A without changing the ten-encounter count. Soren and Chen require player-earned protected Resolve thresholds before authored civilian-danger events trigger voluntary exits. Mixed co-op pursuit supports one rider and one camera-tethered on-foot hero until the next all-on-foot anchor. Assisted clears may earn ordinary progression, separate grades, emblem/title, and unaffected achievements; Extended Protection disables Unbroken Chain/Safe Testimony, Guided Road disables Broken Road, Unbroken Ride, and Extended Grapple disables Together on the Road. First-clear Credits pay once across Standard/Open/Assisted; Continued Runs receive no achievement/economy/grade/prestige/competitive result.
- **Confirmed:** Stage 5 **The Bell Before Dawn / 黎明前的钟声 / Die Glocke vor der Morgendämmerung** uses an **Evidence-Anchored Historical Reconstruction** immediately after Stage 4. Present-day Sheng Guo, Kael, and the neutral archivist compare authenticated records before a dramatized playable past grounded in documents, firsthand memory, and witness corroboration rather than supernatural/VR framing. Canonical first completion permits only young Sheng Guo and Liang Zhen: solo chooses one with authored parallel support/handoffs, co-op assigns both uniquely, and modern heroes are unavailable. Performance cannot alter Oren's betrayal, Rovan's assault, Qiao Lan's sacrifice, collective exile, or Sheng Guo's regret. Return to the present authenticates the Chronicle and unlocks its all-hero non-canonical Mission Remix.
- **Confirmed:** Stage 5 uses the **Five-Act Warning Bell Defense**: Ledger Before Dawn discovers documentary/physical inconsistencies; False Convoy at the Old Trade Entrance exposes concealed weapons and Qiao Lan's non-graphic warning-gate sacrifice; Communal Reserve Defense protects evacuees and essential stores while noncombatant Oren misdirects; Broken Wagon at the Storehouse Platforms gives Liang Zhen his canonical left-leg injury and Sheng Guo's reciprocal rescue; and The Road-Cutter at the Warning Gate requires Rovan's genuine live capture and completes the corroborated evidence chain. A noncombat month-long aftermath covers investigation, mourning, Sheng Guo's individual-judgment proposal, Zhao Heng's rejection, collective exile, and present-day return.
- **Confirmed:** Stage 5 uses a **Four-Ledger Historical Defense Model**: separate trade-worker, reserve-staff and storehouse-evacuee Secure/Strained/Cleared units without death; aggregate essential-reserve Protected/Contested/Secured state with one canonical wagon loss excluded from grading; mandatory Warning Raised plus variable Contained/Strained remaining perimeter while the gate stays damaged; and four indestructible Identified/Authenticated/Committed evidence anchors for Su Yan receipts, concealed weapons, Brann testimony and route/depot records. Qiao Lan's sacrifice and Liang Zhen's post-evacuation permanent injury are fixed canon, not preventable failures; Oren's guilt commits only after post-capture evidence comparison.
- **Confirmed:** Stage 5's **Three-Phase Reciprocal Rescue Handoff** begins Rovan with both historical heroes eligible in Storehouse Interception, integrates evacuation/wagon redirection/Liang's injury/Sheng's rescue in The Broken Wagon, and reserves the last-third Measured Hand Stand for Sheng as the only mobile fighter. Solo Liang transfers deterministically to Sheng; held items enter a one-use support cache. In co-op the Liang player retains bounded non-damaging Field Protector Support through lane warning, gate bracing, one debris redirection and one provision transfer, none mandatory or capable of replacing Sheng's genuine victory. All transition, cache and support state is checkpoint-authoritative.
- **Confirmed:** Stage 5 resolves the solo transition through a **Deterministic Ratio Handoff Contract**. The non-selected solo hero has no hidden combat resources, inventory, collision, damage or checkpoint state before activation. Solo Liang maps his exact remaining Health/Defense/Attack percentages onto Sheng's maxima without refill; both solo routes clear combo/hitstun/cancel/temporary status at the protected transition while preserving score, history, ledgers, eligibility and boss state. The one-use cache contains only Liang's exact equipped stage weapon and one carried provision when present, never dropped/objective/evidence items, and persists claimed state without substitutes. Co-op performs no conversion and each Liang support action remains one-use.
- **Confirmed:** Stage 5's **Complete Historical Hero Kit Contract** gives young Sheng Guo and Liang Zhen fifteen character-owned normals each (7 standing, 3 aerial, 3 ground, 1 dash, 1 low), plus separate throws/defense/breaker/weapons, three initial Specials and fixed Ultimate using the unchanged engine and open future slots. Measured Hand emphasizes lower-damage interruption/redirection/defensive protection; Stone-and-Current emphasizes structure/leverage/takedown/pursuit/front-line control. Both are solo-complete before the injury transition, which never changes Liang's reusable data. First Story completion fixes historical loadouts; Canonical Replay defaults to them, while unlocked Remix/eligible Academy allow legal configuration without rewriting history.
- **Confirmed:** Sheng Guo's fifteen Measured Hand normals are standing **Listening Palm**, **Balanced Elbow**, **Peaceful Release**, **Intercession Step**, **Reserved Reply**, **North Court Turn**, **South Court Pass**; aerial **Rafter Check**, **Descending Measure**, **Courtyard Turn**; ground **Settling Hand**, **Quiet Restraint**, **Measured Rise**; dash **Mediator's Entry**; and low **Threshold Reap**. They prioritize lower-damage interruption, depth/side repositioning, objective protection, safe restraint/separation and counter conversion without an armoured rush; relaunch, stagger/trip, cancel and anti-loop behavior remains within shared budgets and exact data is deferred to Combat Lab.
- **Confirmed:** Sheng Guo's initial techniques are `U` **Civic Arc**, a Resolve-weighted close/rising rescue-space sequence; `Forward + U` **Intervening Passage**, an unarmoured punishable angled carry away from protected space; `Backward + U` **Measured Reversal**, an eligible-strike counter/turn with legal disarm and failed-counter recovery; and fixed `H` **Dawn Bell Accord**, a primary-target interception sequence ending in non-supernatural broad separation with high Resolve, moderate Health damage and optional co-op/rescue continuation. They retain all shared scaling, resistance, cancel, anti-infinite and transition rules.
- **Confirmed:** Liang Zhen's fifteen Stone-and-Current normals are standing **Foundation Palm**, **Turning Forearm**, **Stonecourse Press**, **Advancing Bank**, **Receding Current**, **Skyward Leverage**, **Low Channel Turn**; aerial **Eave Guard**, **Falling Buttress**, **Crossing Current**; ground **Settling Weight**, **Anchor Fold**, **Returning Surge**; dash **Bridge-Line Shoulder**; and low **Riverbed Reap**. They provide stronger direct/guard pressure, structure, leverage, controlled takedown and momentum redirection while retaining movement and shared anti-infinite/relaunch limits, without duplicating Mountain Guardian or River Scout animations, names, timing or routes.
- **Confirmed:** Liang Zhen's initial techniques are `U` **Confluence Guard**, a rooted close/rising defensive-conversion sequence without automatic armour; `Forward + U` **Channel-Break Advance**, a punishable lane-carry stance drive into trip/separation; `Backward + U` **Returning Anchor**, a grounded-advance leverage catch with directional safe throw and failed-counter recovery; and fixed `H` **Stone Guides the Current**, a primary-target brace/redirection/takedown/broad-separation sequence with balanced Health and high guard/Resolve pressure. Presentation is physical and non-supernatural, and all shared legality/budget rules apply.
- **Confirmed:** Stage 5's **False-Convoy Raider Roster** is False Merchant, Torch Saboteur, Wagon Guard and Mounted Raider, with Extreme Route-Cutter Veteran and Ultimate Field Captain. Disguises end at combat; tagged sabotage has interruption/extinguish windows and cannot target people/indispensable evidence; horses safely dismount at zero Stability; Veterans visibly feint and cut lanes without closure; Captains use interruptible formation signals without hidden spawns. Only designated roles use objective actions, capped at one approaching consequence solo/two staggered co-op. Historical tools, horses and wagons replace firearms/modern/sponsor equipment; surrender and live restraint remain.
- **Confirmed:** Rovan's **Three-Phase Road-Cutter** boss uses safe-Stability mounted pole pressure, on-foot short-saber/shortened-pole Convoy Weapon Control containing the wagon injury/rescue transition, and Sheng-only mobile Road-Cutter's Last Stand with strikes, clinches, escape pressure, advanced defense and one breaker while Liang supports. Weapons become evidence rather than pickups; objective results do not change boss stats; no armour/rage/adds/hidden escape/voluntary loss is allowed. Genuine shared-system victory precedes disarm, assessment and live custody, and difficulty adds readable complexity rather than missing tells.
- **Confirmed:** Stage 5's four stage-local pickup families are **Village Gate Staff**, **Carpenter's Work Axe**, **Raider Short Saber**, and **Wagon Hook Pole**. Each has complete Sheng/Liang affinity actions and uses shared `J`, ownership, durability, throw/disarm, anti-infinite, boundary, checkpoint and recovery rules. Staff supplies reach/rescue; Axe handles guard/tagged wood; Saber gives fast non-gory precision/disarm; Hook controls eligible loose cargo/hazards without pulling protected/fixed targets. No pre-equipment, storage or purchase is allowed; Rovan's weapons remain evidence and Qiao's tool remains a Museum artifact.
- **Confirmed:** Stage 5 uses a **Two-Part Historical Transport Contract**. **Reserve Relay Ride** is a two-to-three-minute Chase from the Old Trade Entrance to the Communal Reserve, with one safe-Stability horse per active historical hero, compact mounted controls, a complete on-foot fallback, and exact stow/restore ownership for carried weapons. **Reserve Wagon Passage** is a sixty-to-ninety-second authored moving-platform defense in which players fight aboard or alongside protected supplies without steering; co-op may board together or split, and warned exterior damage affects the Reserve Ledger without permanent destruction. The later Broken Wagon remains a separate fixed hazard, exposed false-convoy wagons become evidence/scenery, and all horses use safe dismount/withdrawal. Transport is stage-local and non-persistent, with no free roam, lethal crash, animal collision weapon, or random-physics outcome; `STEADYRIDE` affects player horses only.
- **Confirmed:** Stage 5 uses a **Deterministic Historical Infrastructure Network** across the Ledger Annex, Old Trade Entrance, Communal Reserve, Storehouse Platforms and Warning Gate. Tagged objects use readable Intact/Damaged/Critical/Broken states, deterministic debris and strict quotas. Fixed sacrifice, injury, wagon loss, gate damage and other unavoidable history cannot penalize; mandatory evidence gives no bonus; no general speed score encourages abandonment. ## 2.1 Artwork policy

- **Confirmed:** Codex will be used to generate the game's pictures and artwork.
- **Confirmed:** Generated images must not contain words, captions, labels, speech bubbles, or other language-dependent text.
- **Confirmed:** Localized titles, dialogue, narration, and subtitles are rendered by the game interface over or alongside the artwork.
- **Proposed:** Art prompts, source/reference images, output dimensions, style notes, and asset revisions should be tracked in an asset manifest so later stages remain visually consistent.

## 3. Heroes and progression

- **Confirmed:** Two heroes are initially selectable.
- **Confirmed:** Additional heroes become available as later stages are added and the story develops.
- **Confirmed:** Every hero has a unique set of attacks and combos.
- **Confirmed:** All heroes share universal movement, interaction, defense, hit-reaction, knockdown, and recovery rules while owning data-defined move lists and combo routes.
- **Confirmed:** The two founding heroes use the **Complementary Guardians** direction. They are distinct people with different village responsibilities and fighting identities, but both remain complete and viable in solo play rather than becoming narrow co-op classes.
- **Confirmed:** The working **Mountain Guardian** archetype is a calm, grounded, protective village craftsperson and waterworks keeper. Their combat identity emphasizes guard reads, close control, grounded routes, throws, stable launch setups, and deliberate power without reducing them to an immobile extreme grappler.
- **Confirmed:** The working **River Scout** archetype is a warm, alert, inventive village messenger and river-path guide. Their combat identity emphasizes mobility, aerial pursuit, repositioning, and converting launches without reducing them to a fragile extreme rushdown character.
- **Confirmed:** Their cooperative identity centers on readable setup, launch, pursuit, handoff, and finisher opportunities while retaining the approved universal co-op rules and independent combo contexts.
- **Confirmed:** Both heroes receive a dependable solo baseline that includes a quick starter, defensive answer, launcher or equivalent vertical-control tool, ground route, and reliable finisher. Character-specific strengths change how those tools function rather than removing essential answers.
- **Confirmed:** Hero Specials and Ultimates use a **Heightened Martial Energy** power language. Combat remains primarily physical, while exceptional techniques may manifest stylized energy, wind, water, pressure waves, impact force, or other character-specific expressions.
- **Confirmed:** Familiar fighting-game functions such as projectiles, rising anti-air attacks, advancing multi-hit spins, reversals, and enhanced signature techniques may be used as mechanical vocabulary. Every move receives original naming, animation, timing, trajectory, audiovisual treatment, tactical trade-offs, and character meaning rather than copying another game's complete technique.
- **Confirmed:** Not every hero must receive the same projectile–rising-attack–spinning-attack package. Each starting kit stays solo-viable while emphasizing the hero's own combat identity, and an Ultimate must add a distinct tactical payoff such as Resolve pressure, crowd control, lane movement, rescue space, or combo conversion rather than functioning only as a higher-damage Special.
- **Confirmed:** The two founders use **Character-Owned Complete Kits** rather than mirrored classic packages or extreme specialization. Their initial Special and Ultimate functions cover essential solo needs through different answers and create distinct freestyle-cancel and cooperative-handoff opportunities.
- **Confirmed:** The Mountain Guardian's initial kit uses the working moves **Stonewake Palm** (`U`), a short broad ground-pressure wave; **Gatebreaker Advance** (`Forward + U`), a committed armored drive; **Rising Rampart** (`Backward + U`), a rising anti-air and reversal; and **Rivergate Oath** (`H`), an intercept-and-launch Ultimate ending in a circular rescue shockwave with strong Resolve pressure.
- **Confirmed:** The River Scout's initial kit uses the working moves **Current Crescent** (`U`), a fast narrow pressure-wave projectile; **Gale Wheel** (`Forward + U`), an advancing multi-hit spinning kick; **Ebbstep Return** (`Backward + U`), an evasive retreat and counterattack; and **Tempest Crossing** (`H`), a projectile-led aerial pursuit and lane-shifting finishing sequence.
- **Confirmed:** The move functions and input relationships are fixed; the working move names may change during final character naming and localization. Exact frame data, damage, meter interaction, invulnerability or armor windows, cancel links, trajectories, hit reactions, and audiovisual treatment remain Combat Lab authoring and tuning work.
- **Confirmed:** Future store techniques and later-hero kits follow a **Function Inspiration Library** policy. Existing fighting games, including *Fatal Fury: City of the Wolves*, may be studied for universal tactical functions such as projectile control, rising anti-air, advancing carry, stance branching, defensive conversion, grabs, aerial follow-ups, and traps. Every shipped technique must be independently authored for *Fight for Peace* with its own village-rooted fiction, name, silhouette, animation sequence, timing, trajectory, hit behavior, audiovisual language, trade-offs, depth-axis behavior, and cooperative applications. Existing move names, character identities, distinctive animations, voice lines, effects, timings, or complete command packages are not copied.
- **Confirmed:** The Function Inspiration Library is an internal design and coverage-audit tool, not an in-game catalogue and not a promise that every observed function will be shipped. A candidate technique is admitted only when it fits a specific hero or enemy identity, fills a demonstrated tactical role, preserves sidegrade balance, and remains readable in the depth-axis cooperative battlefield.
- **Unresolved:** The heroes' final personal names, ages, genders, visual designs, detailed biographies, final purchased Special and directional-Ultimate catalogues, voiced mannerisms, and final weapon-affinity move data remain production character-design decisions. The founders' Representative Vertical Slice normal-action catalogues and teaching routes are confirmed, while **Mountain Guardian** and **River Scout** remain working archetype titles rather than confirmed personal names.

## 4. Game modes

- **Confirmed:** Single-player mode.
- **Confirmed:** Same-device co-op mode.
- **Confirmed:** Local Wi-Fi co-op mode.
- **Confirmed:** The top-level content structure is **Story Mode**, **Mission Mode**, and **Chapter Challenge**. Story Mode contains the authored main campaign and mandatory story-integrated historical Chronicles. Mission Mode contains Character Chronicle, Mission Remix, Curated Missions, and Random Operations. Chapter Challenge is the post-campaign competitive replay destination for eligible authored campaign stages. Academy remains a separate primary learning destination rather than a score-oriented Play mode.
- **Confirmed:** Mission Mode uses one **Archive-led Mission Hub** with separate Character Chronicle, Mission Remix, Curated Missions, and Random Operations destinations. Chronicles are framed through the Village Museum and Archive; Curated Missions use authored civil-protection case records; Random Operations use a civil-protection operations board. Every entry declares **Canonical**, **Canon-compatible Side Story**, **Non-canonical Remix**, or **Generated Operation** before selection. Locked entries provide localized, spoiler-safe clues, and the complete hub supports pointer, keyboard, gamepad, touch, focus indicators, accessibility labels, and screen readers.
- **Confirmed:** Curated Missions launch with four handcrafted missions using different objective structures. They are canon-compatible side stories that may deepen characters and communities without containing mandatory campaign facts, sponsor evidence, endings, required heroes, required techniques, or exclusive required power. Packages are versioned and declare supported solo and co-op configurations. "Curated Missions" is the player-facing term; DLC is a distribution description, while free-versus-paid remains a separate commercial decision.
- **Confirmed:** The initial Curated Mission collection uses four **Protective Duty** objectives: Rescue and Evacuation, Mobile Escort, Recover and Secure, and Nonlethal Apprehension. They may introduce canon-compatible side-story locations, weapons, mounts, environments, enemies, bosses, and new heroes while remaining inside the permanent shared Combat Constitution.
- **Confirmed:** Curated Mission 1 is **Storm over Qinglan**, set approximately 10–14 days after Stage 3 and before Stage 4. Three groups are rescued through short authored evacuation routes after a severe storm while unaffiliated relief looters exploit damaged mountain access. Condition bands affect rank but not survival. The mission adds a storm-damaged pass, cable bridge, runoff, timber cover, deterministic debris hazards, a pickable Rescue Hook Pole, relief-looter variants, a raider-lieutenant boss, and a Qinglan rescue-network Museum record through existing schemas. It contains no Tomas or sponsor evidence and introduces no hero.
- **Confirmed:** *Storm over Qinglan* uses a **Five-Segment Rescue Circuit**: Qinglan Relief Staging Yard; Runoff Road and the traveling clinic team; Storm Cable Bridge and its maintenance workers; Service Tunnel Approach and sheltering residents; and Relief Turnout with final wagon defense and boss. It targets 30–40 minutes for a first clear and 20–28 minutes for replay, with an initial baseline of approximately eight mandatory encounters plus the boss and two optional combat areas. Checkpoints follow each rescued group and precede the boss. Short protected solo interactions and complementary co-op roles preserve equal completion access.
- **Confirmed:** Each Qinglan rescue group uses a shared **Secure, Strained, Critical** condition. Only a completed warned objective attack, unhandled authored hazard, or abandoned active stabilization can remove one band, with a one-band-per-event cap and protection grace. Critical cannot become death or mission failure. The clinic emergency case, bridge secondary anchor, and tunnel reinforcement each provide one contextual one-band recovery or a route-safety bonus when the group is already Secure. Safe-shelter arrival locks condition; solo and co-op receive equivalent completion access; difficulty changes pressure rather than state meaning; and S rank permits a minor recovered mistake.
- **Confirmed:** Qinglan's two optional branches are **Signal Through the Rain / 雨中信号 / Signal im Regen**, restoring a cliffside warning relay for one longer later runoff warning and a route-safety bonus, and **Every Crate Accounted For / 物资一件不少 / Jede Kiste zählt**, recovering ordinary community relief crates for one pre-boss ownership-aware Life, Defense, or Momentum choice per hero. Either expands the **Qinglan Community Rescue Archive / 青岚社区救援档案 / Qinglaner Rettungsarchiv**; both in one eligible run award **Qinglan Stands Together / 青岚同心 / Qinglan hält zusammen**. Cheat-Assisted play may reveal Museum material but grants no branch mastery achievement.
- **Confirmed:** Qinglan's relief-looter core roles are **Pass Brawler**, **Relief Snatcher**, **Hook Looter**, and **Timber Breaker**. Extreme adds **Rainstep Feinter** and Ultimate adds **Crew Caller**. Only Snatchers and Breakers use designated objective actions; solo permits one objective attacker and co-op at most two with staggered warnings. No role can affect a sheltered group. The visibly improvised faction has no firearms, grenades, military armour, sponsor equipment, or shared seal, and uses surrender/restraint outcomes rather than execution.
- **Confirmed:** Qinglan's portable **Rescue Hook Pole** is a `J`-handled long weapon emphasizing pull, spacing, and tagged debris redirection through the shared integrity, affinity, throw, drop, disarm, break, and cheat rules. It supports selected rescue actions but cannot move people, bosses, vehicles, boulders, or indispensable structures. Fixed rescue fittings and bounded deterministic recovery prevent objective softlocks; the pole is neither a pre-stage loadout nor a permanent Store weapon.
- **Confirmed:** Qinglan weather uses deterministic warned runoff lanes, seeded falling-debris patterns, three-state unstable timber with a bounded fragment, authored bridge sway, a recoverable Secure/Threatened/Stabilized anchor, and a relief wagon with protected indispensable cargo. Hazards pause during protected narrative and restoration states, use identical solo/co-op schedules, and retain timing under reduced-motion presentation.
- **Confirmed:** Qinglan's boss is **Mara Venn / 玛拉·文**, age 37, titled **the Storm Jackal / 暴雨豺狼 / der Sturmschakal**. The former salvage and road-recovery leader knowingly organizes independent relief theft without Tomas, Garran, sponsor, or Meridian ties. Her three-phase graph moves from a breakable/disarmable long recovery hook, to short pry-bar and clinch fighting, to one warned wagon-brake attempt whose failure damages only exterior grading state. She obeys all shared boss, Resolve, Defense, Combo Breaker, anti-infinite, co-op rescue, and live-capture rules.
- **Confirmed:** Qinglan uses eight mandatory ordinary encounters plus Mara and two optional branch encounters. The route introduces Brawlers/Snatcher, Hook Looter, clinic Snatcher/Breaker pressure, bridge lane control, anchor defense, tunnel debris fighting, tunnel stabilization, and full-roster wagon defense in order. Initial solo mandatory-route targets are approximately 16/22/25/27/30 ordinary enemies from Easy through Ultimate, with optional enemies outside those totals. Extreme adds Feinter after the first rescue; Ultimate uses Caller before but not during Mara. Co-op adds approximately 35–40 percent ordinary threat without multiplying hazards, thresholds, specialists, objective attackers, or boss support.
- **Confirmed:** Qinglan's recovery set is **Warm Rice Porridge / 热米粥 / Warmer Reisbrei** for Life, **Mineral Citrus Water / 矿物柑橘水 / Mineral-Zitruswasser** for Defense, **Sesame-Nut Bar / 芝麻坚果条 / Sesam-Nuss-Riegel** for Momentum, and **Qinglan Rescue Meal / 青岚救援餐盒 / Qinglan-Rettungsmahlzeit** for balanced recovery. Medium solo uses 2/2/2 plus one balanced meal; Medium co-op 3/3/3 plus one. Easy adds one of each common type; Hard/Extreme/Ultimate remove one/two/three authored common items. The balanced meal remains before Relief Turnout. Objective items are never consumable or replaceable by `FULLPANTRY`, and checkpoints preserve consumption/ownership.
- **Confirmed:** Qinglan's 100-point grade weights Rescue Condition 45, Route/Relief Protection 20, Combat Mastery 25, and Resource/Active-Time Efficiency 10. Final Strained caps rank at A and final Critical at B; recovery to Secure preserves S eligibility. Candidate bands are C clear, B 60, A 75, and S 90 with all groups Secure, validated per difficulty and player count. Optional branches are compensating bonuses, never S requirements. Non-meaningful destruction/repetition cannot score, and Cheat-Assisted clears receive no score, grade record, economy, mastery, or prestige reward.
- **Confirmed:** Qinglan's pickup set is the unique **Rescue Hook Pole**, existing-profile **Wooden Cudgel** drops from Pass Brawlers, and existing-profile **Short Utility Hook** drops from Hook Looters. Mara's reinforced hook and short handle remain boss equipment/evidence and cannot be picked up. The mission contains no firearms, grenades, pre-stage weapons, playable mounts, mounted enemies, horses, or motorbikes. The relief wagon is non-rideable, and `STEADYRIDE` displays **Not Applicable in This Mission**.
- **Confirmed:** Qinglan's compact eight-achievement catalogue is **After the Storm / 风雨之后 / Nach dem Sturm**; the two approved branch achievements; **Qinglan Stands Together**; **Everyone Sheltered / 人人安然 / Alle in Sicherheit**; **Hook, Brace, Rescue / 钩、固、救 / Haken, sichern, retten**; **The Wagon Holds / 救援车屹立 / Der Wagen hält**; and co-op **Guardians in the Downpour / 暴雨守护者 / Wächter im Wolkenbruch**. Co-op is never required for S, Museum completion, or solo rewards. First eligible clear grants Credits, Chronicle, Museum, art, and music; first A grants the text-free Qinglan Rescue Knot emblem; first S grants the Storm Responder title. Cheat-Assisted completion preserves narrative/Museum/media access but grants none of the eight achievements, Credits, rank, emblem, or title.
- **Confirmed:** Qinglan presentation uses a 25–35-second skippable **Storm Warning** opening, in-engine elder briefing/loadout and state-sensitive rescue/boss scenes, and a 30–45-second skippable **After the Storm** coda with bounded condition/branch still inserts. Gao Wen coordinates on site. Generated source has no embedded language content; EN/zh-Hans/DE subtitles and transcripts are complete; both cinematics unlock in the Museum.
- **Confirmed:** Qinglan's **Hands through the Rain** music family contains **Rain over Qinglan**, **Road beneath the Runoff**, **Guide Them Home**, **The Storm Jackal**, and **Shelter Lights**. They never reward delayed rescue or intentional damage, remain maximum three/one per family, and grant no gameplay reward.
- **Confirmed:** Qinglan uses four recovery checkpoints after clinic shelter, bridge shelter/finalized anchor, tunnel shelter, and immediately before Mara. Snapshots preserve every group, recovery, branch, objective, hazard index, environment state, enemy outcome, hero resource, item/weapon, score/time/grade, achievement/Highlight, cheat, and network-recovery fact without reroll, repair, duplication, or state improvement. Results store the complete four-part objective ledger and configuration under separate comparable categories; replays use tiered Curated retention; validated technical recovery may preserve eligibility, while ordinary continues follow shared rules and remain recorded.
- **Confirmed:** Qinglan first use provides a replayable localized **Rescue Operations** card and safe demonstrations. Installing the case adds an optional **Storm Rescue Drill** covering interruption, stabilization, Hook redirection, and shelter arrival without exclusive power or access gating. Objective states/warnings support icon, silhouette, text, audio, captions, screen reader, current bindings, non-pointer operation, and independent rain/lightning/sway/particle/shake/reflection reductions. Stabilization supports hold/toggle/reduced-input forms; simulation-changing help uses Assisted records.
- **Confirmed:** Qinglan supports applicable hero cheats plus `QUIETCROWD`, `FULLPANTRY`, `HOMECOMING`, and carried-weapon `TRUSTYTOOLS`. `STEADYRIDE` and `SAFEPASSAGE` display Not Applicable because the case has no mount and no completion-blocking protected-target loss. - **Confirmed:** Curated Mission 2 is **The Medicine Road**, set after Stage 5's present-day investigation and before Stage 6. Four established heroes protect a compact clinic cargo vehicle, clear obstructions, defend two repair stops, recover marked supply cases, and use bounded motorbike pursuit sections against an independent road gang. Convoy and cargo condition affect results while the essential delivery remains canonical. The mission adds a forest road, service tunnel, stone viaduct, remote clinic, Tire Iron, Folding Road Baton, Road Blockers, Bike Harriers, Cargo Snatchers, ranged Lookouts, and a mounted-to-dismounted road-gang boss through existing schemas. It contains no Tomas or sponsor evidence and introduces no hero.
- **Confirmed:** *The Medicine Road* uses a **Five-Link Medical Corridor** through Council Medical Dispatch Yard, Forest Switchbacks, Old Service Tunnel, Stone Viaduct and Service Turnout, and Remote Clinic Approach. It targets 30–40 minutes first clear and 20–28 experienced replay, with about eight mandatory ordinary encounters plus a mounted-to-dismounted boss. The two repair stops address a wheel assembly and cargo-restraint frame; bounded moving defenses, case recovery, road clearance and bike pursuit occur between authored secure anchors, and players never steer the clinic vehicle. Condition, repairs, recovery, collateral and combat affect results while essential delivery remains canonical and independent of Tomas/sponsor evidence.
- **Confirmed:** *The Medicine Road* uses a **Four-Part Medical Delivery Ledger**. Clinic Vehicle Condition is Roadworthy, Damaged or Stabilized and can never explode, cause pedestrian harm, be permanently destroyed or fail the mission. Six marked cases—two medicine, two diagnostic-equipment and two emergency-supply—each track Secured/Displaced/Recovered custody and Intact/Scuffed seal condition; they cannot be consumed, opened, destroyed or permanently lost, an exiting carrier moves a case to a fixed later recovery point, and recovery locks it against repeat removal. The wheel assembly and cargo-restraint frame independently track Ready for Repair, Interrupted and Completed; interruptions pause rather than reset work, the protected mechanic has no ordinary Health/death, solo receives a protected repair window and co-op may divide repair and defense. Route obstructions track Blocked/Being Cleared/Reopened with no hidden timer and always have normal and fixed-fallback clearance. Only warned designated actions alter the ledgers; solo allows one approaching cargo threat and co-op at most two staggered threats against different cases. Exact state persists through checkpoints, essential delivery always succeeds, and preventable incidents reduce score and recognition.
- **Confirmed:** *The Medicine Road* uses a **Deterministic Condition and Bike Handoff Contract**. Mandatory road-stress repairs are condition-neutral; only warned designated vehicle impacts change Roadworthy to Damaged, and Damaged becomes Stabilized only after both repairs complete. The vehicle is protected from further condition change after CP3. Cases start Secured/Intact; completed removal makes Displaced, interruption before escape permits Intact recovery, and a warned completed escape/rough-transfer moves the case to its fixed recovery point and makes it permanently Scuffed. Ordinary physics/network events never Scuff it, and one continuous case sequence counts as one S-rank incident. Each hero has one persistent run bike: intact parked bikes carry exact Stability through anchors, zero-Stability bikes are never repaired/replaced, and all later mounted sections including Luo remain fully completable on foot; co-op bikes are independent.
- **Confirmed:** *The Medicine Road* uses a **Coordinated Road-Gang Roster**: slow Road Blockers guard and construct tagged barriers; Bike Harriers make bounded motorbike passes and safely dismount at zero Stability; Cargo Snatchers use a visible interruptible channel against one marked case, carry only one and cannot retarget a Recovered-locked case; and Ridge Lookouts use finite blunt sling projectiles with visible warnings, may interrupt an unprotected repair channel, and cannot damage cargo, vehicle or mechanic off-screen. Extreme adds a Tow-Chain Enforcer limited to tagged loose barriers and obstacles, never people, cases, vehicles, bosses or indispensable structures. Ultimate adds an interruptible Road-Gang Dispatcher who coordinates one existing Harrier or Snatcher action without hidden spawns or unavoidable combinations. Only designated Blocker and Snatcher actions alter objectives; solo allows one approaching objective action and co-op two staggered actions against different targets. Difficulty raises coordination rather than Health. All enemies permit surrender/live restraint and use no firearms, grenades, military gear or sponsor insignia; `QUIETCROWD` cannot remove selected advanced roles or the boss.
- **Confirmed:** *The Medicine Road* boss is **Luo Jian / 罗坚**, a **Three-Phase Roadmaster**. Road Closure Run uses a mid-class motorbike, telegraphed passing attacks and a separate Stability bar, with safe on-foot counter windows and tagged-barrier redirection if heroes dismount or lose their bikes. Baton Interception uses a reinforced folding road baton with complete standing/aerial/low/ground routes; a legal disarm or Health threshold removes it, but at least one bounded phase decision cycle must occur. Cornered Leader is a complete empty-hand phase with rough boxing, shoulder checks, low kicks, short throws, Exact Blocks and one defense-consuming Combo Breaker. Co-op restraints always permit partner rescue. Shared anti-infinite, ground, defense and difficulty rules apply, with no armour, rage immunity, reinforcements, infinite guard or objective immunity. Live restraint and medical assessment follow defeat; Luo Jian confirms independent resale motive and no Tomas/sponsor connection before canonical delivery.
- **Confirmed:** *The Medicine Road* uses a **Medical-Corridor Impact Network**: empty transport objects and loading frames at Dispatch Yard; timber barriers, maintenance crates, route posts and drainage pipes at Forest Switchbacks; tool carts, maintenance fencing, cable reels, cabinets and safe lighting controls in Old Service Tunnel; sandbags, cable spools, removable barriers and nonlethal guardrails on Stone Viaduct; and benches, empty supply carts, water containers and awning supports at Remote Clinic Approach. - **Confirmed:** Temporary Guest mode participates fully during the session but cannot store permanent progression. The results screen states exactly what each persistent profile recorded and whether it completed a global unlock requirement.
- **Confirmed:** Progression ownership never modifies combat simulation or grants host gameplay advantages. Disconnect, rejoin, and incomplete-session credit are defined by the later local-Wi-Fi recovery rules.
- **Confirmed:** Local-Wi-Fi synchronization uses a **Host-Authoritative Deterministic Simulation with Prediction and Bounded Rollback**. Solo, hot-seat, host, and client all use the same fixed-step gameplay simulation, provisionally targeting 60 simulation updates per second.
- **Confirmed:** Devices exchange frame-stamped abstract gameplay actions rather than raw device events. The host owns authoritative frame order, accepted inputs, random seed, spawn decisions, and canonical gameplay state while every peer simulates the same combat, AI, items, weapons, and encounter rules.
- **Confirmed:** Each device predicts its local player's input immediately and uses a small configurable input delay to absorb ordinary Wi-Fi variation. Late remote inputs inside an authored rollback window rewind and deterministically resimulate affected frames.
- **Confirmed:** Peers exchange periodic state hashes. On divergence, the host supplies a compact authoritative snapshot; severe visible correction is deferred to a safe presentation point when possible without allowing simulation disagreement to continue.
- **Confirmed:** Gameplay state is rollback-safe. Particles, camera shake, and similar presentation effects remain outside canonical state, while audiovisual events use stable identifiers so resimulation cannot replay the same hit, voice, or effect multiple times.
- **Confirmed:** Network quality monitoring may raise input delay within an authored safe range before declaring the session unstable. Solo and hot-seat omit networking and rollback overhead while retaining the same deterministic combat rules.
- **Confirmed:** Combat Lab network tests include artificial latency, jitter, packet loss, rollback, state-hash divergence, snapshot recovery, and duplicate-presentation-event detection.
- **Confirmed:** Local-Wi-Fi interruption recovery uses **Short Recovery Grace, Restricted Survival Proxy, and Host Migration**. A briefly missing player does not immediately end the encounter or hand unrestricted control to AI.
- **Confirmed:** As soon as remote input is judged missing, held movement and repeated inputs are cleared. The hero completes any already committed action and returns to a guarded neutral state during a short recovery grace.
- **Confirmed:** If the interruption outlasts that grace, a restricted survival proxy may follow the active hero, avoid hazards, block, evade, recover, and use conservative basic attacks when necessary. It cannot spend Super meter, initiate cooperative attacks, consume or throw items, collect or throw weapons, or make route, story, difficulty, progression, or other player-owned choices.
- **Confirmed:** The proxy uses the disconnected hero's real Health and Defense resources and receives no invulnerability. Encounter rules remain stable until an authored encounter or checkpoint boundary rather than immediately rescaling around the missing player.
- **Confirmed:** A returning peer receives and validates an authoritative snapshot, then regains control at the first safe actionable moment rather than interrupting a proxy action midway.
- **Confirmed:** A persistent guest who remains disconnected through the result receives only already confirmed statistics and checkpoint credit, not the final clear, rank, or unlock. A short result-screen recovery grace may still restore full completion credit when validation succeeds.
- **Confirmed:** If the host disconnects, the guest promotes its last confirmed deterministic frame to authority, discards unconfirmed frames through bounded rollback, and continues as the new host. A returning former host rejoins as the guest.
- **Confirmed:** When migration or resynchronization cannot be validated, the game keeps each profile's already confirmed statistics and checkpoint credit (no continuable checkpoint, see the 2026-10-31 save decision) and ends the network session with a clear explanation instead of guessing or granting unverified completion.
- **Confirmed:** Disconnect, proxy-control, migration, snapshot, rejoin, and credit events are recorded in the deterministic session log for replay, debugging, and progression verification.
- **Confirmed:** Local-Wi-Fi session discovery uses **Dual-Path Ephemeral Pairing**. The normal path creates a short-lived room code and QR code through a minimal signaling service; an offline fallback exchanges the complete WebRTC pairing package manually through two-way QR scanning or copy-and-paste.
- **Confirmed:** Pairing never requires a player account. Online room codes and pairing tokens are single-use, expire quickly, and carry only the temporary offer, answer, and connection-candidate messages needed to establish the session; they do not store gameplay, saves, or progression.
- **Confirmed:** After pairing, deterministic gameplay messages use a WebRTC `RTCDataChannel` peer connection whenever the browsers and network can establish it. Online and offline pairing feed the same host-authoritative gameplay protocol and do not create different combat rules.
- **Confirmed:** Before the lobby becomes ready, both peers validate the game version, networking-protocol version, deterministic-rules hash, and installed content manifest. A mismatch blocks the match with a specific corrective explanation rather than risking desynchronization.
- **Confirmed:** The lobby displays both selected heroes, assigned input devices, difficulty, connection quality, content compatibility, and readiness. The host can revoke the unstarted pairing, and each participant explicitly confirms readiness before gameplay begins.
- **Confirmed:** Offline pairing waits for candidate gathering and packages the required offer or answer and connection candidates into one transferable payload. The interface may split an oversized QR payload into an ordered sequence while always retaining copy-and-paste as an accessibility and device fallback.
- **Confirmed:** Pairing failures distinguish expired codes, incompatible versions or content, camera-permission problems, malformed payloads, unreachable peers, and restrictive or client-isolated Wi-Fi. The game does not promise universal zero-input LAN discovery from browser APIs.
- **Confirmed:** Pause and browser-focus recovery use a **Deterministic Courtesy Hold with Bounded Recovery**. Solo and same-device play pause immediately when the game window loses focus, becomes hidden, or an assigned controller disconnects.
- **Confirmed:** In local-Wi-Fi play, unexpected focus loss or page hiding first clears held inputs and requests a short synchronized courtesy hold at the latest mutually confirmed simulation frame, provisionally about three seconds. Canonical combat timers and resources stop advancing while connection monitoring and recovery messages remain active.
- **Confirmed:** A player who returns during the courtesy hold must explicitly confirm readiness. Both devices resume from the same confirmed frame after a synchronized countdown; merely making the tab visible again never restarts combat automatically.
- **Confirmed:** When the courtesy hold expires, the session enters the approved interruption-recovery sequence instead of remaining frozen indefinitely: recovery grace, restricted survival proxy, or host migration as applicable.
- **Confirmed:** Either local-Wi-Fi participant may request an explicit synchronized pause, which begins at a deterministic safe boundary. During it, players may change local controls, audio, subtitle, display, and accessibility settings, but cannot change difficulty, combat rules, progression ownership, or other shared match conditions.
- **Confirmed:** Pausing clears stale gameplay inputs and does not generate meter, reduce cooldowns, age defensive timing windows, advance AI, or create any other simulation advantage. Resume begins from neutral input state after the synchronized countdown.
- **Confirmed:** Combat Lab reliability tests cover window blur, tab hiding, minimization, controller loss, repeated visibility changes, host suspension, courtesy-hold expiry, manual pause, synchronized resume, and transition into proxy or migration recovery.
- **Confirmed:** Performance handling uses **Fixed Simulation with Independent Adaptive Presentation**. The deterministic combat simulation retains its validated fixed rate, provisionally 60 Hz, while each device renders independently at the frame rate it can sustain.
- **Confirmed:** Presentation may omit or interpolate visual frames without dropping, stretching, or redefining simulation frames. Exact Block windows, counters, cancels, hitboxes, movement, AI, cooldowns, and all other gameplay timing remain identical across display refresh rates and quality settings.
- **Confirmed:** Auto Quality is enabled by default, with manual quality levels available. Automatic reductions follow an authored order across decorative background animation, nonessential particles and debris, post-processing, lighting effects, and internal rendering resolution.
- **Confirmed:** Adaptive quality never removes or delays gameplay-critical silhouettes, attack telegraphs, directional warnings, HUD information, subtitles, hit confirmation, or essential sound cues.
- **Confirmed:** Simulation catch-up work is bounded so a slow device cannot enter an unlimited backlog spiral. Temporary performance debt lowers presentation quality first, then uses bounded catch-up or an authoritative snapshot when necessary.
- **Confirmed:** Both devices run a short simulation and rendering preflight before a local-Wi-Fi match. Persistent performance failure triggers a synchronized courtesy hold and a clear diagnostic rather than silently slowing combat.
- **Confirmed:** Lobby and progression ownership are separate from technical simulation authority. If the authoritative device remains unstable while its peer is healthy, authority may migrate to the healthier device without changing difficulty access, save ownership, or completion-credit ownership.
- **Confirmed:** If neither device can sustain the validated simulation requirements, the session keeps already confirmed statistics and checkpoint credit (no continuable checkpoint, see the 2026-10-31 save decision) and ends safely instead of corrupting synchronization or changing combat speed.
- **Confirmed:** Combat Lab performance tests cover 30, 60, 120, and 144 Hz presentation, artificial CPU load, dropped render frames, bounded catch-up, thermal slowdown, resizing, fullscreen changes, independent quality transitions, authority migration, and dual-device failure.
- **Deferred:** Local Wi-Fi synchronization is implemented after the solo and same-device combat simulations are stable.
- **Unresolved production values:** Random Operations and Chapter Challenge feature models are complete; their exact content catalogues, encounter compositions, score and rank thresholds, and balance values remain production data governed by their approved validation matrices. Final simulation rate validation, minimum simulation headroom, presentation-quality thresholds and reduction order, catch-up limits, preflight duration and pass criteria, authority-performance scoring, initial and maximum input delay, rollback-window size, input-prediction policy, hash cadence, snapshot format, safe correction points, network-quality thresholds, concrete `RTCDataChannel` configuration, signaling-service implementation, pairing-payload format and QR limits, optional relay policy, heartbeat timeout, recovery-grace duration, proxy behavior and cooldowns, migration timeout, result-screen recovery grace, checkpoint-credit boundaries, snapshot/rejoin presentation, courtesy-hold duration, safe pause boundary, resume acknowledgements and timeout, manual-pause limits, and exact focus-loss policy remain networking implementation and test values. The Host-Authoritative Deterministic Simulation with Prediction and Bounded Rollback, Short Recovery Grace with Restricted Survival Proxy and Host Migration, Dual-Path Ephemeral Pairing, Deterministic Courtesy Hold with Bounded Recovery, and Fixed Simulation with Independent Adaptive Presentation models are confirmed.

## 5. Platform and input

- **Confirmed:** The game runs in a web browser.
- **Confirmed:** Keyboard and gamepad input are supported.
- **Confirmed:** The initial Player 1 keyboard concept is:
  - `W/A/S/D`: movement and directional modifiers.
  - `T`: attack and directional attack/combo inputs.
  - `Z`: jump and aerial attack/combo inputs.
  - `U`: character-specific special moves, modified by direction.
  - `G`: guard-system actions, modified by direction where applicable.
  - `H`: super attack when the attack meter permits it.
  - `J`: grab, throw, pick up food, pick up weapons, and throw equipped weapons.
- **Confirmed:** The listed keyboard bindings are defaults rather than fixed controls.
- **Confirmed:** Players can reassign keyboard and gamepad controls in the game settings.
- **Confirmed:** Default letter bindings follow the character printed by the active keyboard layout. For example, the German QWERTZ key labeled `Z` triggers Jump even though its physical browser code is `KeyY`.
- **Confirmed:** Physical keyboard keys and gamepad controls map to a shared set of abstract gameplay actions. Combat rules do not depend directly on a particular device or key.
- **Confirmed:** Special moves use a **Five-Slot Progressive Special Layout**. Neutral `U` always performs the hero's fixed signature Special and cannot be replaced.
- **Confirmed:** `Forward + U` and `Backward + U` begin with hero-specific core Specials and may later be reassigned to compatible purchased Specials. `Up + U` and `Down + U` begin empty and may be filled with compatible purchased Specials.
- **Confirmed:** Purchasing or reassigning a Special never deletes the hero's original moves. Owned Specials may be re-equipped without another purchase, and an empty optional slot performs no substitute attack.
- **Confirmed:** `Forward` and `Backward` are resolved relative to the hero's facing when the `U` input is accepted. `Up` and `Down` retain their stage-depth meaning, and Assisted Facing cannot silently redirect the selected Special.
- **Confirmed:** Direction-plus-button inputs use a deterministic cardinal-direction resolver shared by keyboard and gamepad. Keyboard ties favor the most recently pressed cardinal direction; analog input uses the dominant axis after dead-zone processing. Exact dead zones and equal-axis tie behavior remain Combat Lab tuning values.
- **Confirmed:** Special assignments are saved per persistent profile and hero as part of named loadouts. The last-used loadout remains selected until the player changes it.
- **Confirmed:** Ultimate moves use a **Progressive Five-Choice Ultimate Loadout with a Shared Full Meter**. Neutral `H` always performs the hero's fixed signature Ultimate and cannot be replaced.
- **Confirmed:** `Forward + H`, `Backward + H`, `Up + H`, and `Down + H` are initially empty assignable slots for compatible purchased Ultimates. The inputs exist from the beginning, while their move content is added progressively rather than requiring five Ultimates per hero at launch.
- **Confirmed:** Every Ultimate choice uses the same Attack Bar. Activating any Ultimate consumes the full bar and makes all other Ultimate inputs unavailable until the bar is refilled; one Ultimate cannot cancel or chain directly into another.
- **Confirmed:** Directional Ultimates are tactical sidegrades rather than upgrades to the signature move. They may emphasize advancing assault, defensive reversal or rescue, depth repositioning, lane control, area pressure, or another authored role with explicit trade-offs.
- **Confirmed:** Each acquired Ultimate declares compatible directional slots and cannot occupy multiple slots in the same loadout. Owned Ultimates are permanent and may be re-equipped without another purchase.
- **Confirmed:** An empty directional Ultimate slot does not fall back to neutral `H`, preventing an unintended Attack-Bar expenditure. The game supplies brief audiovisual feedback when a full-meter directional input addresses an empty or incompatible slot.
- **Confirmed:** Ultimate direction resolution uses the same deterministic input rules as Specials: the direction is captured when `H` is accepted, forward/backward use the hero's facing at that moment, up/down retain stage-depth meaning, and Assisted Facing cannot change the selected slot.
- **Confirmed:** Browser compatibility uses **Tested Support Tiers with Capability Gates**. The game is desktop-first because its primary controls require a keyboard or gamepad.
- **Confirmed:** Tier 1 browsers receive complete validation for solo, same-device co-op, local-Wi-Fi co-op, keyboard, gamepad, audio, saves, rendering, and performance. The initial Tier 1 goal covers current desktop Chrome, Edge, Firefox, and Safari, with exact supported versions published and revised through the compatibility test process.
- **Confirmed:** A Tier 2 browser may run the game when its capability preflight succeeds, accompanied by a clear best-effort support notice. Mobile browsers and embedded webviews are not initially promised complete gameplay support.
- **Confirmed:** Compatibility is evaluated by required capabilities rather than browser-name rejection alone. The preflight independently checks rendering, Web Audio, keyboard and gamepad input, browser database storage, WebRTC data channels, page visibility, optional camera access, and optional fullscreen support.
- **Confirmed:** Missing optional capabilities disable or replace only their dependent feature. For example, denied camera access preserves copy-and-paste offline pairing, and missing fullscreen support preserves windowed play.
- **Confirmed:** Missing WebRTC disables local-Wi-Fi mode without blocking solo or same-device play. A missing capability required by the selected mode produces a localized explanation before a stage begins.
- **Confirmed:** Browser-specific workarounds remain isolated behind platform adapters and cannot alter deterministic combat data, timing, hit detection, AI, progression, or other gameplay rules.
- **Confirmed:** Combat Lab compatibility validation combines an automated browser report with manual controller, audio-latency, fullscreen, save-storage, and local-network tests on the published Tier 1 matrix.
- **Unresolved:** Exact input-buffer and chord windows, analog dead zones, equal-axis tie behavior, per-hero Special- and Ultimate-slot compatibility, initial directional-Ultimate inventory, empty-slot feedback, browser-version ranges, operating-system coverage, minimum rendering capability, Tier 2 warning language, mobile experimentation, and the compatibility-test device matrix remain implementation and validation decisions. The Five-Slot Progressive Special Layout, Progressive Five-Choice Ultimate Loadout with a Shared Full Meter, and Tested Support Tiers with Capability Gates models themselves are confirmed.

## 6. Combat system

### 6.1 Combat categories

- **Confirmed:** Standing attacks and combos.
- **Confirmed:** Aerial attacks and combos.
- **Confirmed:** Ground attacks and combos.
- **Confirmed:** Character-specific special moves.
- **Confirmed:** Super attacks require a full attack meter.
- **Confirmed:** The attack meter is refilled through successful attacks and combos.

### 6.2 Ground combat

- **Confirmed:** Ground combat uses the **downed follow-up** model.
- **Confirmed:** Heroes can perform brief attacks, grabs, or submission-style actions against a knocked-down enemy.
