- **Confirmed:** Empty-fire input produces bounded dry feedback but no hit confirmation, meter, combo continuation, or repeatable sound spam. Firearms use ammunition rather than per-shot integrity as their primary depletion; throws, hazards, clashes, or authored events may still destroy them.
- **Confirmed:** `TRUSTYTOOLS` prevents applicable integrity loss but does not preserve or restore ammunition. No infinite-ammunition code is added to the initial cheat catalogue. Any future code must explicitly create Cheat-Assisted status and join the full cheat verification matrix.
- **Confirmed:** Higher difficulties preserve player weapon damage, controls, ammunition behavior, and defense rules while changing armed-enemy intelligence, contested pickup placement, optional acquisition routes, and authored combinations under the Threat Budget.
- **Confirmed:** Presentation is stylized, readable, and non-gory. Firearms are temporary protective tools rather than glamorous permanent equipment. Every critical warning uses at least two available channels, and players can reduce flashes, shake, recoil motion, explosive brightness, transient loudness, particles, and vibration without removing essential cues.
- **Confirmed:** Implementation follows **Architecture Hooks Now, Playable Content Later**. The Stage 1 architecture keeps extensible weapon, ammunition, targeting, cancel, ownership, checkpoint, and network schemas, but production encounters arrive in later stages. Optional placeholder Lab work is not a vertical-slice gate.
- **Confirmed:** Later validation covers deterministic targeting, ammunition, pickup arbitration, transfers, checkpoint state, combo scaling, resistance, environmental triggers, cheats, replay, local-Wi-Fi synchronization, reconnect recovery, accessibility, and performance before final media production.
- **Unresolved:** Exact later-stage weapon models, first introducing stage, ammunition counts, shot and burst timing, damage, enemy roles, pickups, resupplies, routes, artwork, sound, and encounters remain future content and Combat Lab decisions. The advanced-weapon system model is confirmed.

### 7.4 Later-stage mounted and vehicle encounters

- **Confirmed:** Horses, motorbikes, wagons, and comparable riding systems are **post-Stage 1 authored encounters**, not universal free-roaming systems or pre-stage equipment. Stage 1 may reserve schema extension points or use optional placeholder Lab tests, but mounts are not part of its acceptance scope.
- **Confirmed:** Mounted encounters use shared abstract controls plus an authored profile. Directions steer; `T` attacks; `Z` jumps or performs an evasive maneuver where supported; `G` performs the profile's defense; `U` performs a mounted Special; and a compatible full-meter `H` performs a mounted Ultimate.
- **Confirmed:** A **Chase** profile assists forward motion and emphasizes lane, pace, and lateral adjustment. An **Arena** profile permits bounded free movement. An **Escort** profile keeps participants within a managed range around a protected objective. All profiles honor remapping, current key labels, gamepad input, and optional steering assistance.
- **Confirmed:** Every mount supplies shared movement, collision, defense, and basic attack rules. Every hero receives a compact mounted identity kit: neutral `T`, a directional attack, an eligible jump or evasive attack, the encounter's `G` response, one hero-specific mounted `U`, and an eligible mounted `H`.
- **Confirmed:** Purchased on-foot moves do not automatically work while mounted. A later technique may declare a balanced mounted-compatible variant. Dismounting restores the hero's saved on-foot loadout unchanged.
- **Confirmed:** Hero Health, Defense, and Attack Bar keep their exact values across mounting and dismounting. A mount or vehicle adds the only new encounter resource, **Stability**.
- **Confirmed:** Direct attacks declare whether they affect Hero Health, Defense pressure, Stability, or a readable combination. Collisions primarily reduce Stability; dangerous crashes may also damage Health. Stability can recover through clean riding, defense, evasion, or a short damage-free interval according to the encounter profile.
- **Confirmed:** At zero Stability, the rider enters a telegraphed critical state. A further qualifying hit causes an authored crash or forced dismount rather than an unexplained instant failure. An encounter may continue on foot or restart from a nearby riding checkpoint according to its declared profile.
- **Confirmed:** Mounted `G` may map to a Defense-consuming vehicle guard or brace, a committed movement-based evasion, or an explicitly compatible Exact Block or Counter. It never changes a universal timing window silently.
- **Confirmed:** Mounted attacks build the normal Attack Bar and a compatible `H` consumes the full bar. Mounting never refills Health, Defense, or Attack Bar.
- **Confirmed:** Separate riders own separate Stability values. A shared vehicle owns one shared Stability value visible to both players. Checkpoints save roles, mount state, Stability, objectives, and the exact persistent hero resources.
- **Confirmed:** Co-op encounter configurations are authored as **Parallel Riders**, **Driver and Fighter**, or **Escort Formation**. Role changes occur only through safe authored transitions or a deliberate swap command, and both roles receive meaningful mechanics, scoring opportunity, and feedback.
- **Confirmed:** Single-player uses deterministic companion behavior, contextual commands, or designated role switching. Same-device co-op always permits both players to act simultaneously; local-Wi-Fi uses the same canonical vehicle state and recovery protocol.
- **Confirmed:** A crash or disconnect by one participant does not automatically fail the other. Encounters provide an authored catch-up, rescue, survival proxy, or checkpoint result consistent with the existing interruption rules.
- **Confirmed:** Mounts enter through story-authored temporary access such as borrowed horses, captured motorbikes, or a defended wagon. Mount and dismount points are clearly marked. Mounts cannot occupy hero loadout slots or be used in unsupported areas.
- **Confirmed:** Store content may provide cosmetic mount variants or explicitly balanced riding-sidegrade content, but never permanent access that bypasses authored encounter boundaries.
- **Confirmed:** Mounted opponents use a dedicated roster of pursuers, blockers, flankers, boarders, ranged harassers, support drivers, elites, and phased bosses as appropriate. Dismounted survivors may continue on foot when authored; the system does not multiply every ordinary enemy by every vehicle.
- **Confirmed:** Mounted bosses use Resolve, safe phase transitions, and authored crash windows. A single collision cannot bypass a boss phase or eliminate the boss outside an explicitly valid conclusion.
- **Confirmed:** Riding reuses a mounted-compatible subset of the environmental system. Only tagged objects interact at speed; capped speed and force may open routes, slow pursuers, create co-op opportunities, or reduce Stability. Ordinary high-speed fragments remain cosmetic unless explicitly authored as cargo or a mounted weapon.
- **Confirmed:** Mounted scoring contributes to the normal stage result and may receive an optional section grade for clean riding, Stability, rescues, cooperation, takedowns, and routes. Mounted grades never block story or difficulty access and do not lock essential combat power.
- **Confirmed:** Cheat-Assisted riding follows the existing reward restrictions. It may progress story and difficulty access but awards no score, Credits, grade, achievements, or mastery rewards.
- **Confirmed:** `SOFTERBLOWS` reduces Hero Health damage but not Stability damage. `SAFEHEART` prevents Health loss but not Stability loss or a safe forced dismount. `STEADYFEET` changes eligible ordinary knockdowns but not an authored mount crash. `IRONGUARD` prevents eligible Defense cost but not Stability damage. `BRIGHTSPIRIT` supplies the ordinary full Attack Bar. `STRONGHANDS` doubles outgoing Health and Resolve damage but not Stability, objective damage, speed, or collision force. `TRUSTYTOOLS` does not protect a mount. `QUIETCROWD`, `FULLPANTRY`, and `HOMECOMING` retain their declared scopes.
- **Confirmed:** First exposure uses a localized control card and safe practice segment. Academy later provides optional mounted lessons. Steering assistance, slower speed, wider warnings, lane correction, and independent motion/effect settings are explicit options rather than silent automation.
- **Confirmed:** Production uses **One Complete Vertical Slice, Then Expand**. The first candidate is a motorbike pursuit that tests speed, lane movement, attacks, Stability, obstacles, co-op roles, checkpoints, deterministic replay, all input devices, local-Wi-Fi synchronization, accessibility, performance, and difficulty behavior with placeholder art first.
- **Confirmed:** Horseback, wagon, and further vehicle content is produced only after the common mount, encounter-profile, move, enemy-role, obstacle, objective, save, replay, and networking contracts pass validation. This later vertical slice is not the Stage 1 Representative Vertical Slice.
- **Unresolved:** The introducing stage, final first mount, exact movement and Stability values, mounted move data, compatible purchased moves, enemy roster, boss, scoring bands, assistance thresholds, artwork, sound, and encounters remain later-stage production and Combat Lab decisions. The mounted encounter system model is confirmed.

## 8. Enemies and bosses

- **Confirmed:** Each stage ends with a boss.
- **Confirmed:** Bosses can use standing, aerial, and ground combos.
- **Confirmed:** Bosses can pick up and use weapons.
- **Confirmed:** Later stages add new enemy types, bosses, and attack patterns.
- **Confirmed:** Stage 1 uses a **Layered Faction Ecology** rather than a uniform raider army or a sequence of named duelists. Combat behavior distinguishes frightened or desperate participants from committed aggressors while preserving readable brawler crowds and fighting-game depth.
- **Confirmed:** The Stage 1 core roster contains five working archetypes: **Pressed Recruit** for basic pressure and morale behavior; **Bridge Shield** for defense and lane control; **Canal Runner** for flanking and aerial pressure; **Rope Overseer** for grappling and forced repositioning; and **Sling Lookout** for ranged control with authored warnings.
- **Confirmed:** Coerced workers remain protected non-combatants. Pressed Recruits may lose morale, disengage, flee, or surrender and cease being legal targets; committed Overseers, Lookouts, and other aggressors continue according to their authored behavior.
- **Confirmed:** A sponsor-equipped **Black-Iron Enforcer** serves as a selected challenge enemy on Hard and higher tiers. It adds an advanced capability and formation problem rather than merely increasing statistics, and its presence supports the outside-sponsor story hook.
- **Confirmed:** The Stage 1 boss uses the working role title **Sluice Commander**. The Commander is a multi-phase exam of the same standing, aerial, ground, defense, Resolve, anti-infinite, weapon, and surrender rules used elsewhere; phases may include empty-handed combat and claiming the Canal Workstaff or Iron Gate Handwheel.
- **Confirmed:** The defeated Sluice Commander may surrender or be captured rather than executed. The role labels and final personal identity remain content-authoring choices, but the roster composition and gameplay responsibilities are fixed.
- **Confirmed:** Enemy groups coordinate committed offense through a **Shared Threat Budget with Tactical Roles** rather than fully independent aggression or strict one-at-a-time attacker slots.
- **Confirmed:** Each offensive action has an authored threat cost based on its danger, range, tracking, and required defensive response. The actor reserves that cost on commitment and releases it after recovery, interruption, or cancellation.
- **Confirmed:** Positioning, circling, weapon pursuit, and other non-offensive movement do not consume attack budget. Enemies awaiting permission continue to flank, reposition, threaten, or prepare instead of visibly standing idle.
- **Confirmed:** Low-cost pokes may create limited overlapping pressure, while grabs, launchers, heavy attacks, ranged control, and other high-impact actions reserve more of the shared budget.
- **Confirmed:** The scheduler rejects overlapping attack combinations whose timing, directions, or response requirements leave no reasonable defense. Tactical roles such as pressure, flanker, ranged, grappler, and support influence which eligible enemy receives attack permission.
- **Confirmed:** Authored group attacks reserve a larger combined budget for every participant and use a distinct shared telegraph. Other major attacks cannot overlap that sequence unless the encounter explicitly provides compatible counterplay.
- **Confirmed:** Co-op encounters enforce both a global encounter budget and per-hero pressure limits so the enemy group cannot arbitrarily spend all pressure on one player. Player-count scaling adjusts the budget deliberately rather than simply doubling it.
- **Confirmed:** Boss encounters may reserve part of the threat budget for the boss while supporting enemies share the remainder. Difficulty may tune budget, recovery, role selection, and move availability, but enemies never read player inputs.
- **Confirmed:** Attack permission and ties resolve from deterministic simulation state, preserving identical crowd behavior across solo, hot-seat, and local-Wi-Fi play.
- **Confirmed:** Co-op targeting uses **Role-Aware Target Scoring with Commitment**. Each enemy scores valid heroes using distance and path availability, tactical role, current pressure distribution, recent interactions, weapon suitability, and encounter objectives.
- **Confirmed:** Reaching a hero's per-hero pressure limit strongly penalizes additional targeting rather than enforcing an artificial equal split. Pressure and grappler roles favor reachable close targets, flankers favor open approach angles, ranged roles favor clear lanes, and support roles favor useful positions around allies.
- **Confirmed:** Recent damage may influence target selection but never creates a universal forced-retaliation rule. Low health also does not automatically attract every enemy; only explicitly authored hunter-style behavior may give vulnerability additional weight.
- **Confirmed:** After selection, an enemy maintains a minimum target-commitment period to prevent rapid switching. It cannot switch during a committed attack, combo, grab, or recovery unless the current target becomes invalid.
- **Confirmed:** Commitment may end early when the target is defeated, disconnected, unreachable, outside the combat region, or no longer compatible with the enemy's current role. Confirmed protected re-entry and other invulnerable states are respected when choosing legal attacks.
- **Confirmed:** Target changes are communicated through facing and movement before the next committed attack. Boss phases may adjust scoring priorities but obey the same valid-target and commitment rules.
- **Confirmed:** Target scoring runs at fixed simulation intervals and uses stable tie-breakers, preserving deterministic behavior in solo, hot-seat, and local-Wi-Fi play.
- **Confirmed:** Enemy defense uses **Tiered Capability Profiles with Shared Rules**. Every enemy participates in the same combat state machine, legal windows, resource costs, anti-infinite protections, and response vocabulary, but each archetype receives an intentional subset of defensive abilities.
- **Confirmed:** Basic enemies normally receive Normal Block plus one simple recovery behavior. Defensive specialists may receive stronger directional guard and Exact Block; counter specialists receive a clearly telegraphed Counter family rather than every possible response.
- **Confirmed:** Grapplers, ranged enemies, weapon users, and other tactical roles receive defenses that reinforce their identity. Elites may combine several advanced options, constrained by Defense resources, recovery commitments, and authored cooldowns.
- **Confirmed:** Bosses may use the broadest defensive toolkit, but access changes visibly by phase and never bypasses normal resource costs or legal activation windows.
- **Confirmed:** Enemy Combo Breakers consume Defense and obey the same state gates, anti-infinite rules, and committed recovery as hero breakers. Capability profiles and cooldowns prevent enemies from chaining Exact Block, Counter, and Breaker without a punishable opening.
- **Confirmed:** Defensive AI uses an authored perception delay and current visible action state; it never reads future player inputs. Difficulty may improve decision quality and reduce reaction delay only within authored fair limits, not grant impossible frame-perfect knowledge.
- **Confirmed:** A compact Defense indicator appears when a non-boss enemy can actively spend defensive resources; boss Defense remains persistently readable. Academy and Combat Lab identify each archetype's defensive capability profile.
- **Confirmed:** Defensive choices, resource use, and cooldowns resolve deterministically rather than through unsynchronized random rolls.
- **Confirmed:** Bosses prevent indefinite stun-locking through a **Visible Resolve Gauge with Earned Stagger Windows** while continuing to use the heroes' core standing, aerial, ground, defensive, and co-op combat vocabulary.
- **Confirmed:** Valid hits deal ordinary Health damage plus authored Resolve damage. Heavy finishers, Counters, Specials, Supers, weapon clashes, and co-op techniques may receive higher Resolve damage values.
- **Confirmed:** While Resolve remains, the boss still shows hit impact and limited reactions but resists full launch, extended ground routes, and submission states unless an action explicitly overrides that resistance.
- **Confirmed:** Depleting Resolve produces a clearly telegraphed **Stagger Break** during which the boss becomes vulnerable to standing, aerial, ground, and co-op combo routes.
- **Confirmed:** Boss-specific juggle, stun, bounce, and damage-scaling budgets remain active during Stagger Break. The window ends through its authored duration, exhausted combo budget, or a valid concluding finisher and knockdown.
- **Confirmed:** Stagger Break suppresses autonomous boss Combo Breakers by default so the earned opening remains meaningful. Any rare phase-specific exception must be authored explicitly and telegraphed before it can occur.
- **Confirmed:** After Stagger Break recovery, the boss receives brief protected re-entry and refills Resolve according to the current phase. Resolve never secretly refills while the boss remains in an active combo.
- **Confirmed:** Phase changes may alter Resolve capacity or resistance tags, but HUD state and reactions communicate those changes. Co-op attacks may build and exploit the same Stagger Break without bypassing team-combo limits.
- **Confirmed:** Resolve damage, depletion, recovery, and all state transitions are deterministic and exposed in Combat Lab diagnostics.
- **Confirmed:** Boss phase thresholds use a **Queued Transition with Capped Damage Carryover**. The threshold-crossing hit resolves completely and marks the boss `phasePending` instead of interrupting the current contact or granting immediate invulnerability.
- **Confirmed:** A pending boss remains in its current legal hit reactions until the combo naturally ends, a valid finisher or knockdown concludes it, or an authored maximum transition-grace limit is reached. Health is clamped at the current phase floor during this period.
- **Confirmed:** Additional valid damage accumulates in a visible carryover pool up to an authored cap that cannot reach the following phase threshold. When the pool is full, later contacts may finish the route but do not deal health damage or generate exploitable self-refunding Attack meter.
- **Confirmed:** At the first legal safe point, the transition begins and stored damage is applied when the next combat phase becomes active. If the grace limit expires during an exceptional route, the current hit still resolves before a telegraphed forced separation.
- **Confirmed:** Both heroes enter safe actionable states before the new phase starts. Supers and co-op finishers spend their resources normally even when they trigger a threshold.
- **Confirmed:** Active hazards, projectiles, grabbed weapons, loose pickups, and supporting enemies use explicit phase-transition policies rather than being silently deleted or reset. The complete threshold, carryover, and transition sequence is deterministic in every multiplayer mode.
- **Confirmed:** Enemy attack initiation uses **Visibility-Gated Attacks with Authored Warnings**. The simulation defines an authoritative combat visibility zone in world coordinates, so legality never depends directly on browser resolution or CSS viewport size.
- **Confirmed:** Melee attacks, grabs, sudden dashes, and untelegraphed launchers must begin inside the combat-safe portion of that zone. Hidden enemies may reposition, pursue, collect weapons, and prepare entrances without beginning those offensive actions.
- **Confirmed:** Ranged attacks, reinforcements, and arena-wide boss techniques may begin outside the zone only when explicitly tagged `offscreenCapable`; each requires an authored minimum warning plus a directional edge indicator and distinct audio cue using non-color-only communication.
- **Confirmed:** An attack begun legally while visible may continue if momentum carries its actor partly outside the zone, but it cannot gain new tracking or reverse toward a hero while hidden. Newly spawned enemies cannot arrive with an active damaging hitbox unless the entrance is itself a clearly telegraphed stage hazard.
- **Confirmed:** Off-screen-capable actions reserve their complete threat-budget cost. Boss arena attacks may use wider authored visibility rules, but their danger regions and valid defensive responses remain readable.
- **Confirmed:** Every client renders warnings from the same simulation-defined zone even when display crops differ. Eligibility, warning timing, and resolution remain deterministic across solo, hot-seat, and local-Wi-Fi play.
- **Unresolved:** Exact encounter budgets, per-action threat costs, per-hero pressure limits, role-selection and target-scoring weights, target-commitment duration, early-release thresholds, hunter-style vulnerability rules, permitted overlap combinations, group-attack reservations, boss/add budget splits, per-archetype defensive capability assignments, perception delays, defense cooldowns, resource values, Defense-indicator presentation, boss Resolve capacity and per-action damage, resistance overrides, Stagger Break duration and budgets, protected re-entry timing, phase refill rules, phase-health thresholds, carryover caps, transition-grace limits, forced-separation behavior, phase policies for active entities, visibility-zone dimensions, combat-safe margins, off-screen-capable assignments, warning durations and presentation, and difficulty modifiers remain enemy-module and Combat Lab tuning values. The Shared Threat Budget with Tactical Roles, Role-Aware Target Scoring with Commitment, Tiered Capability Profiles with Shared Rules, Visible Resolve Gauge with Earned Stagger Windows, Queued Transition with Capped Damage Carryover, and Visibility-Gated Attacks with Authored Warnings models themselves are confirmed.

## 9. Difficulty and unlocks

- **Confirmed:** Easy, Medium, and Hard are initially available.
- **Confirmed:** Completing Hard unlocks Extreme.
- **Confirmed:** Completing Extreme unlocks Ultimate.
- **Confirmed:** A campaign completion made under the declared **Cheat-Assisted** ruleset may unlock the next global difficulty tier. A Cheat-Assisted Hard completion may therefore unlock Extreme, and a Cheat-Assisted Extreme completion may unlock Ultimate, allowing less-experienced players to encounter the authored enemies, variants, formations, and behaviors unique to those profiles.
- **Confirmed:** Difficulty uses **Authored Multidimensional Profiles** rather than numerical scaling alone. Player controls, move properties, cancel rules, Exact Block windows, Counter inputs, and other learned hero mechanics remain identical across every difficulty.
- **Confirmed:** Enemy Health and damage may change only within bounded authored ranges. Difficulty also controls threat budget, pressure limits, AI perception delay, role-selection quality, defensive-option frequency, recovery commitment, move selection, encounter composition, and resource placement.
- **Confirmed:** Essential warnings and valid defensive counterplay are never removed. Higher profiles may use the tightest validated warning duration but cannot introduce unreadable attacks, hidden input reading, arbitrary armor, or impossible reactions.
- **Confirmed:** Food, weapons, checkpoints, and other recovery resources use authored difficulty placements rather than random punishment. Solo/co-op scaling is applied separately from difficulty so two-player mode does not simply double every enemy value.
- **Confirmed:** Easy emphasizes generous resources, lower simultaneous pressure, longer enemy decision delays, and reduced use of advanced routes. Medium is the reference experience and primary balance target.
- **Confirmed:** Hard adds stronger coordination, broader enemy move selection, and modest statistical increases. Extreme adds advanced encounter combinations, authored group attacks, fewer resources, and more efficient defensive decisions.
- **Confirmed:** Ultimate uses the fullest enemy capability profiles, most demanding fair coordination, and tightest validated recovery opportunities. Extreme and Ultimate create challenge through new patterns and combinations rather than inflated statistics alone.
- **Confirmed:** Difficulty remains stable throughout a run; the game does not secretly adapt upward or downward based on performance. The selection screen explains behavioral and resource differences, and Combat Lab can expose and compare every active profile value.
- **Confirmed:** Difficulty rosters use a **Core Roster with Advanced Variants and Selected Challenge Enemies** rather than five completely separate enemy casts or one unchanged roster.
- **Confirmed:** Easy uses a reduced selection of each stage's core enemies and gentler combinations. Medium presents the complete story-critical roster and remains the canonical narrative experience.
- **Confirmed:** Hard introduces elite variants, alternate weapon loadouts, expanded move sets, and earlier use of advanced core enemies. Extreme introduces a small number of challenge-only archetypes or major variants with genuinely new mechanics.
- **Confirmed:** Ultimate uses the full roster, master variants, rare team combinations, and advanced behaviors rather than requiring another entirely separate art roster.
- **Confirmed:** Difficulty variants must change moves, defenses, tactical roles, equipment, or group behavior rather than only Health and damage. They require distinct silhouettes, equipment, animation cues, and localized names; palette changes alone are insufficient.
- **Confirmed:** No story-essential character, boss, or plot information is exclusive to Extreme or Ultimate. Challenge-only enemy bestiary and Academy entries unlock after the player encounters them, and every added enemy remains consistent with its stage's theme and story.
- **Confirmed:** Stage data defines difficulty-specific roster substitutions and wave variants without altering the shared combat engine.
- **Confirmed:** Difficulty access uses **Permanent Global Tiers with Per-Stage Mastery Records**. Completing the currently released main campaign on Hard unlocks Extreme for the player profile; completing it on Extreme unlocks Ultimate.
- **Confirmed:** A global tier unlock is permanent and is never revoked when later stages are released. Newly added stages may immediately be played on every globally unlocked tier, while the game may recommend learning a new stage on Medium or Hard without imposing another gate.
- **Confirmed:** Difficulty access and challenge recognition are separate. A Cheat-Assisted completion may advance story and global tier access, but it does not create a Standard, Open, or ordinary Assisted difficulty clear, rank, score, competitive record, Hero Mastery award, Credit payout, or skill-based Difficulty achievement.
- **Confirmed:** Every stage separately records highest completed difficulty, score, rank, best combo, and solo/co-op completion. Difficulty-specific enemy, bestiary, and Academy discoveries still require encountering that content in the relevant stage.
- **Confirmed:** Save data stores the global unlocked tier independently from installed-stage manifests, so content updates cannot silently change or remove an earned unlock. Unlock requirements and progress are displayed explicitly.
- **Confirmed:** A co-op lobby may select a difficulty unlocked by the host. Participating profiles receive their own stage-completion records; detailed guest tier advancement is defined by the co-op progression rules.
- **Confirmed:** Profile persistence uses **Versioned Transactional Saves with Portable Backups**. IndexedDB is the canonical local store for persistent profiles, while downloaded backup files provide browser-independent and device-independent recovery.
- **Confirmed:** Each persistent profile has a stable identifier and stores progression, difficulty unlocks, per-stage mastery, discoveries, records, settings, and Academy combo recipes separately from cached game assets.
- **Confirmed:** Persistent profiles also store the versioned cheat registry state, reusable hero-specific cheat preferences, revealed activation cards, separate highest Cheat-Assisted completion difficulty, and canonical identifiers for Cheat-Assisted records. These fields participate in transactional saves, migration, export, import, and recovery snapshots.
- **Confirmed:** Important changes are saved atomically at confirmed checkpoints, result validation, unlocks, settings changes, and explicit Academy saves. The system never relies on a page-unload event as its only chance to preserve progress.
- **Confirmed:** Each profile retains its current snapshot and at least one previous known-good snapshot. Snapshots include a schema version, content-version context, timestamp, and integrity checksum.
- **Confirmed:** Save-schema migrations are explicit and tested. The original snapshot remains available until migration succeeds; corruption recovery restores the newest valid prior snapshot and tells the player what was recovered.
- **Confirmed:** The game requests persistent browser storage when available but does not claim that browser storage can never be cleared. Private or incognito sessions display a clear warning that permanent progression may not survive.
- **Confirmed:** Players can export a versioned Fight for Peace profile-backup file and import it in another supported browser or device. Import validates the file, previews the profile and records it contains, and offers an explicit compatible-record merge or selected-profile replacement.
- **Confirmed:** Import never silently overwrites a profile. An automatic recovery snapshot is created before replacement, and incompatible or newer save schemas fail safely with a localized explanation.
- **Confirmed:** Cloud accounts and cloud saves are not required. A later optional synchronization service must use the same portable save model rather than replacing or trapping local profiles.
- **Confirmed:** Only one browser tab may actively write a given profile; additional tabs remain read-only until the player explicitly transfers ownership. In local-Wi-Fi play, each device writes only its own verified profile results.
- **Confirmed:** Save failures are reported visibly and retain the last confirmed snapshot instead of presenting uncommitted progression as successfully stored.
- **Confirmed:** The progression store uses **Separated Cosmetics with Sidegrade Combat Loadouts**. Score measures performance within one stage attempt, while Credits are persistent, profile-owned currency earned only through gameplay rather than purchased with real money.
- **Confirmed:** Progression uses a **Shared Credit Wallet with Non-Spendable Hero Mastery**. Every persistent profile has one Credit balance usable across all unlocked heroes and store categories; the game does not create separate spendable currencies for individual heroes.
- **Confirmed:** Every hero has an independent Mastery track that records experience and accomplishments with that hero. Hero Mastery is progression, not currency: it cannot be spent, transferred, lost, or converted into Credits.
- **Confirmed:** Playing any hero can earn shared Credits, while only the hero personally used receives Hero Mastery progress. In co-op, each persistent participant gains Mastery for their own selected hero and receives Credits under the confirmed cooperative payout rules.
- **Confirmed:** Hero Mastery may reveal that hero's techniques, acquisition challenges, cosmetics, lore, Academy lessons, and records. Once revealed, an eligible technique still follows the confirmed dual-path acquisition rules.
- **Confirmed:** Shared Credits allow a player to prepare for a newly unlocked hero, but authored reveal milestones require meaningful exposure to that hero before advanced hero-specific content becomes available. This preserves character learning without adding another wallet or forcing repetitive hero-specific currency farming.
- **Confirmed:** Verified stage results, ranks, achievements, Academy trials, discoveries, and authored challenges may award Credits. Difficulty and performance may improve rewards, while repeated low-risk farming is bounded so mastery remains more valuable than repetition.
- **Confirmed:** Visual appearance and gameplay modifiers occupy independent selections. Players may combine any owned cosmetic outfit with any owned combat style instead of being forced to wear a particular appearance for its statistics.
- **Confirmed:** A combat style is a transparent sidegrade with explicit benefits and costs, such as increased Defense with reduced movement speed. Styles cannot enlarge Exact Block or Counter windows, rewrite universal cancel rules, or alter authored attack frame timing.
- **Confirmed:** Purchased combat styles can be equipped in normal solo, same-device co-op, local-Wi-Fi co-op, and Academy play. The Academy additionally permits a controlled try-before-buy preview and teaches the style's trade-offs and routes.
- **Confirmed:** Every hero begins with a complete and viable core move set. Additional Specials and Ultimate attacks may be acquired through the confirmed Credit or mastery paths after their authored reveal conditions are met.
- **Confirmed:** Additional Specials and Ultimates use **Revealed Dual-Path Acquisition**. A technique first becomes visible through an authored story, discovery, or hero-progression milestone; story-sensitive techniques remain concealed until their reveal condition is met.
- **Confirmed:** Once revealed, the player may permanently acquire the technique either by paying its published Credit price or by completing its dedicated Hero Mastery or Academy challenge. The player is never required to pay Credits after successfully completing the acquisition challenge.
- **Confirmed:** Every revealed gameplay technique can be tested without charge in a controlled Academy trial before either acquisition path is chosen. Trial access does not grant ownership or permit the move in ordinary stages.
- **Confirmed:** Purchased and challenge-earned versions are mechanically identical. Neither acquisition path grants stronger properties, and required gameplay techniques do not depend on high-difficulty clears.
- **Confirmed:** Purchasing a technique does not remove its mastery challenge. A player may complete that challenge later for its record and a nonessential authored reward such as a badge, title, cosmetic, or achievement, but not a second copy of the move.
- **Confirmed:** Unlockable moves are alternative tactical tools rather than unconditional upgrades. Heroes equip the confirmed five-slot Special and five-choice Ultimate loadouts before a stage; alternatives differentiate routes, range, risk, resource cost, positioning, defense, or cooperative utility.
- **Confirmed:** A saved per-hero loadout contains the selected cosmetic outfit, combat style, five Special-slot assignments, and current Ultimate assignment or assignments. Players may maintain named loadouts, and the last-used loadout persists until deliberately changed.
- **Confirmed:** Loadouts may be edited in the pre-stage briefing, co-op lobby, eligible between-stage preparation points, and Academy. They cannot be changed during an active encounter.
- **Confirmed:** Store purchases are explicit and deterministic. There are no loot boxes, random purchases, or hidden statistical effects; cosmetics are previewable and combat content is testable in the Academy before purchase.
- **Confirmed:** Stage score records preserve the hero, combat style, Special and Ultimate loadout, difficulty, mode, and relevant content version used for the run. Any later competitive leaderboard separates Standard Loadout and Open Loadout categories.
- **Confirmed:** Score comparison uses **Parallel Standard and Open Loadout Categories** selected before a stage. Each category maintains separate personal records and, if later implemented, separate leaderboards.
- **Confirmed:** A Standard Loadout uses the hero's default combat style, original neutral/forward/backward Specials, empty up/down Special slots, fixed signature neutral Ultimate, and empty directional Ultimate slots. The gameplay loadout remains locked for the run.
- **Confirmed:** Visual-only cosmetics are permitted in Standard. Authored stage pickups, food, weapons, and temporary items are also permitted because they are shared parts of the stage rather than persistent loadout advantages.
- **Confirmed:** An Open Loadout permits any legally owned combat style and any compatible owned Special and Ultimate assignments. The exact loadout and content version remain attached to the result.
- **Confirmed:** In co-op, both heroes must use Standard Loadouts for the team result to qualify as Standard. If either participant selects Open, the team result is recorded as Open.
- **Confirmed:** Difficulty and solo/co-op record divisions remain separate within both loadout categories. Balance or content updates preserve historical results with their recorded version instead of silently rewriting or deleting them.
- **Confirmed:** Credits, owned cosmetics, owned styles, owned moves, and saved loadouts belong to the persistent profile and are included in transactional saves and portable profile backups. Temporary Guest mode cannot retain purchases or currency.
- **Confirmed:** Long-term accomplishment tracking uses a **Layered Record Book with Achievement Tracks**. Records automatically preserve best measured performance, while achievements are authored goals completed once for recognition and possible rewards.
- **Confirmed:** Each stage records personal bests by difficulty and mode, including highest score and rank, fastest clear, highest completed difficulty, best combo damage and hit count, lowest damage taken, solo/co-op completion, selected hero, equipped combat style and moves, and relevant game/content version.
- **Confirmed:** Achievement tracks are organized into Story, Stage Mastery, Hero Mastery, Combat Technique, Discovery, Co-op Teamwork, Academy, and Difficulty categories. Later stages and heroes extend these data-defined tracks without requiring a new achievement engine.
- **Confirmed:** Cheat-Assisted play may grant explicitly narrative Story achievements and persistent discoveries, but cannot grant Stage Mastery, Hero Mastery, Combat Technique, Co-op challenge, Academy challenge, or Difficulty achievements whose meaning depends on validated play. Cheat-Assisted results never overwrite Standard, Open, or Assisted best records.
- **Confirmed:** Most achievements are visible with localized descriptions and progress counters. Secret achievements are reserved for genuine optional discoveries, never conceal essential progression, and do not become permanently missable.
- **Confirmed:** Completing a higher-difficulty achievement also completes an equivalent lower-difficulty version. Achievement goals favor discovery, skill, cooperation, and meaningful persistence rather than long repetitive grind counts.
- **Confirmed:** Co-op achievements reward assists, handoffs, rescues, coordinated routes, and shared finishers without encouraging item theft, partner abandonment, score competition, or other behavior harmful to the team.
- **Confirmed:** Achievement rewards may include Credits, cosmetics, store availability, combat-style availability, access to purchase an advanced move, and profile badges or titles. Achievements never apply hidden permanent statistical bonuses, and essential story content never depends on obscure achievements.
- **Confirmed:** Record and achievement progress belongs to each persistent profile, participates in transactional saves and portable backups, and is granted only from validated gameplay events. Temporary Guest mode may display session accomplishments but cannot retain them permanently.
- **Confirmed:** Credit earning uses a **Layered Result Payout**. A validated results screen itemizes stage-completion, difficulty, rank, first-clear, achievement or challenge, discovery, Academy, and hero-mastery components rather than converting raw score directly into currency.
- **Confirmed:** Score influences Credits only through authored rank thresholds and explicit challenge conditions. Ordinary enemies do not repeatedly drop persistent Credits, preventing hit, spawn, or score loops from becoming currency exploits.
- **Confirmed:** Stage completion supplies a dependable base payout, harder difficulties apply a moderate authored bonus, and final rank contributes a bounded performance bonus. First-clear, discovery, achievement, and equivalent one-time bonuses cannot be collected repeatedly.
- **Confirmed:** Repeated clears may still earn bounded completion and rank rewards, but there are no daily-login rewards, real-time waiting systems, rotating multipliers, or fear-of-missing-out bonuses.
- **Confirmed:** Economy pacing uses **Transparent Effort Bands with Stable Replay Rewards**. Store prices use consistent low, medium, and high effort bands based on content category and scope rather than artificial rarity, player history, or inferred purchasing behavior.
- **Confirmed:** Small cosmetics and profile items normally occupy the low band; full outfits, combat styles, and Specials normally occupy the medium band; directional Ultimates and elaborate prestige cosmetics normally occupy the high band. An item's price never implies that a gameplay option is mechanically stronger.
- **Confirmed:** Prices do not increase because the player previously purchased related items. There are no personalized prices, dynamic prices, temporary sales, or escalating purchase chains.
- **Confirmed:** First clears, discoveries, achievements, and mastery milestones provide the largest one-time Credit bonuses. A representative first-stage clear should support at least one meaningful early purchase under normal play rather than requiring immediate farming.
- **Confirmed:** Repeat clears provide a smaller but stable completion-and-rank payout that does not decay by day, session, or recent repetition. Authored anti-farming limits prevent low-risk exploit loops without making legitimate stage replay cease to reward the player.
- **Confirmed:** Higher difficulties accelerate acquisition moderately without changing store prices or becoming mandatory. High-band gameplay items require meaningful progress but are never balanced around numerous identical low-risk clears; the mastery-challenge path remains a valid alternative.
- **Confirmed:** A failed run receives no completion, difficulty, or rank payout, but retains already validated one-time achievements and discoveries. Credits are never removed for defeat, restart, loadout changes, or weapon repair.
- **Confirmed:** Every gameplay-affecting combat style and move can eventually be acquired through Easy or Medium play. Higher difficulties accelerate Credit earning but do not monopolize combat content; difficult achievements may still award prestige cosmetics and profile recognition.
- **Confirmed:** In co-op, each persistent profile receives the full shared completion, difficulty, and team-rank payout rather than splitting one reward pool. Personal achievement, Academy, discovery, and hero-mastery bonuses are evaluated separately.
- **Confirmed:** Last hits, item or weapon collection, and individual score never reduce a partner's Credit payout. Disconnected-player rewards follow the verified completion-credit rules, while Temporary Guest mode cannot retain currency.
- **Confirmed:** Store availability uses a **Progressive Permanent Catalogue**. Items are revealed as the player encounters relevant heroes, stages, enemies, weapons, mechanics, achievements, and mastery milestones; once revealed, an entry never rotates away or expires.
- **Confirmed:** The store has no timers, limited-time offers, rotating inventory, expiring prices, or artificial scarcity. Locked entries show their Credit price and exact prerequisite whenever that information does not spoil unrevealed story content.
- **Confirmed:** Story-sensitive entries remain hidden or appear as deliberately undisclosed entries until the relevant encounter. Players may mark revealed locked entries as goals and track the required achievement, mastery condition, and Credit amount.
- **Confirmed:** Store content is grouped into Wardrobe for cosmetics, Training Hall for combat styles, Technique Library for Specials and Ultimates, and Profile Collection for badges, titles, gallery content, and related profile rewards.
- **Confirmed:** Cosmetics can be previewed on the selected hero. Combat styles display every benefit and penalty, while Specials and Ultimates display inputs, meter costs, tactical purpose, and compatible loadout slots.
- **Confirmed:** Gameplay-affecting content supports controlled Academy trials before purchase. Catalogue state clearly distinguishes owned, available, locked, and mastered content.
- **Confirmed:** After purchase, the player may equip the item immediately or retain the current loadout. Ownership is permanent for the profile, participates in backup/export, and is never revoked by later balance changes.
- **Confirmed:** New stages and heroes extend the catalogue through data-defined entries and reveal conditions without replacing the store system or removing existing inventory.
- **Confirmed:** Every newly added stage contributes two new visual-only Wardrobe outfits for every hero available through that stage. A newly introduced hero therefore launches with one default costume plus two Credit-purchasable alternatives, while previously available heroes receive two new alternatives tied to the new stage.
- **Confirmed:** The recurring pair uses two character-specific themed families: one **Stage Service Outfit** connected to the new stage's location or civic role and one **Village Heritage Outfit** expressing cultural, festival, ceremonial, or personal-life identity.
- **Confirmed:** Each counted outfit includes meaningful clothing-design changes rather than being only a palette swap, while preserving combat readability, animation anchors, hitbox-independent gameplay, and the hero's recognizable identity. Palette-only variants may exist as additional low-band cosmetics but do not replace either promised outfit.
- **Confirmed:** These outfits grant no statistics or combat effects, remain independent from combat styles, are eligible in Standard Loadouts, use transparent Credit prices, become permanently available after their reveal condition, and participate in profile backup and export.

### 9.1 Village Museum and Archive

- **Confirmed:** The campaign uses a persistent **Village Museum and Archive** as the unified destination for previously referenced gallery content, recovered collectibles, and replayable audiovisual material. It expands after each stage rather than creating separate stage-specific gallery systems.
- **Confirmed:** The Museum is reached from the Village Command Menu and uses a visually presented two-dimensional room-and-exhibit interface with fast category navigation. It is not a fully walkable gameplay environment and remains equally usable with keyboard, gamepad, or pointer input.
- **Confirmed:** The Museum contains a **Picture Gallery** for story, character, location, and costume artwork; a **Cinema Room** for unlocked introductions, interludes, briefings, endings, and optional clips; a **Music Hall** for unlocked music; a **Sound Workshop** for a curated set of notable hero, enemy, weapon, environmental, and interface sounds; a **Collections Hall** for recovered artifacts; and an **Investigation Desk** linking to the Sponsor Dossier.
- **Confirmed:** The Sound Workshop is curated rather than exposing every minor technical variation. Museum entries may provide zoomable artwork, playback controls, localized names, provenance, short descriptions, subtitles, narration choices, transcripts, sound captions, and accessibility settings appropriate to their media type.
- **Confirmed:** Story media unlocks when its canonical trigger is reached even when the player skips playback. Discoveries and artifacts unlock through their authored conditions. Future-stage Museum pages do not appear before the relevant stage becomes available, preventing unreleased names, locations, or collection counts from becoming spoilers.
- **Confirmed:** Profile Collection store rewards open in or link to the Museum rather than forming a competing gallery. The Sponsor Dossier retains its confirmed-fact and current-theory rules, including its prohibition against revealing the planned mystery structure through empty clue slots or fixed hidden counts.
- **Confirmed:** Museum progression stores stable unlock identifiers, viewing state, and collection records in the persistent profile; large pictures, video, music, and sound files remain installed game assets rather than being duplicated inside browser saves. Unlock state participates in transactional saves, migration, backup, and import.
- **Confirmed:** In cooperative play, an authored shared collectible recovered by either hero unlocks for both participating persistent profiles. Temporary Guest mode may view session discoveries but cannot retain them permanently.
- **Confirmed:** Cheat-Assisted play may unlock Museum media, story entries, and persistent discoveries under the existing cheat rules, but it cannot grant Stage Mastery or other validated-skill achievements merely by collecting them.
- **Confirmed:** Museum collectibles are heritage, evidence, commemorative, or safely recoverable display objects; heroes do not remove critical working equipment or irreplaceable resources merely to fill an exhibit. Medical and technical entries remain fictionalized and non-actionable where safety requires it.
- **Confirmed:** Museum content is data-defined so later stages can extend categories, exhibits, localization, and unlock conditions without replacing the system. Only media with recorded commercial-use rights and asset provenance may ship in the Museum.

The Stage 1 Layered Faction Ecology, Authored Multidimensional Difficulty Profile, Core Roster with Advanced Variants and Selected Challenge Enemies, Permanent Global Tiers with Per-Stage Mastery Records, Versioned Transactional Saves with Portable Backups, Separated Cosmetics with Sidegrade Combat Loadouts, Shared Credit Wallet with Non-Spendable Hero Mastery, Revealed Dual-Path Acquisition, Parallel Standard and Open Loadout Categories, Layered Record Book with Achievement Tracks, Layered Result Payout, Transparent Effort Bands with Stable Replay Rewards, and Progressive Permanent Catalogue models themselves are confirmed.

## 10. Combat Lab

- **Confirmed:** The Combat Lab is the first sub-project and is used to validate the game's combat foundation before Stage 1 is produced.
- **Confirmed:** Combat Lab features will be refined one module at a time.
- **Confirmed:** Refinement combines written decisions with a continuously updated low-fidelity browser prototype, allowing gameplay decisions to be evaluated through both explanation and live testing.
- **Confirmed:** Planned modules are:
  1. Input and movement.
  2. Basic offense.
  3. Hero identity and unique combos.
  4. Aerial combat.
  5. Ground combat.
  6. Defense.
  7. Resources.
  8. Grabs and throws.
  9. Weapons.
  10. Enemy behavior.
  11. Boss rules.
  12. Co-op combat.
  13. Browser reliability.
- **Proposed:** The first playable Combat Lab milestone ends after Modules 1–6. Later modules build on the validated core.
- **Proposed:** The Lab provides input history, state readouts, hitbox/hurtbox visualization, frame timing, damage and combo data, configurable training enemies, instant reset, slow motion, and frame stepping.

### 10.1 Module 1 — Input and Movement

- **Confirmed:** Ground navigation uses free belt-scrolling movement.
- **Confirmed:** `A/D` move horizontally and `W/S` move through scene depth under the default Player 1 keyboard mapping.
- **Confirmed:** Diagonal ground movement is supported.
- **Confirmed:** The same movement actions are available through a gamepad directional pad or analog stick.
- **Confirmed:** Players can choose Manual Facing or Assisted Facing independently in the game settings.
- **Confirmed:** Manual Facing is the default.
- **Confirmed:** In Manual Facing, a neutral attack uses the hero's current facing; an explicit left/right attack input turns the hero and attacks in that direction.
- **Confirmed:** In Assisted Facing, a neutral offensive attack can turn toward an appropriate nearby enemy.
- **Confirmed:** An explicit left/right attack input always overrides Assisted Facing.
- **Confirmed:** Assisted Facing does not change targets during an active combo. Retargeting is allowed only after the combo ends or the player deliberately changes direction.
- **Confirmed:** Assisted Facing applies to neutral offensive attacks; it does not automatically control blocking, exact blocks, counters, or escape movement.
- **Confirmed:** Facing mode does not change attacks, damage, timing, meters, or combo rules.
- **Confirmed:** Each local co-op player can select a facing mode independently.
- **Proposed:** Facing assistance and simplified move inputs remain separate settings if an additional simplified input style is introduced later.
- **Confirmed:** Normal ground movement uses a consistent walking speed rather than device-dependent analog walk/run speed.
- **Confirmed:** Double-tapping any direction performs a short dash in that direction, including horizontal, depth, and diagonal directions.
- **Confirmed:** `Dash` is an abstract input action that players can optionally bind to a dedicated keyboard key or gamepad button instead of using the double-tap gesture.
- **Confirmed:** Dashing does not provide invulnerability and therefore does not replace defensive actions.
- **Confirmed:** Pressing jump without a direction performs a neutral jump.
- **Confirmed:** Holding a direction when jumping establishes the initial aerial movement direction.
- **Confirmed:** Players can make limited directional corrections while airborne but cannot instantly reverse aerial momentum.
- **Confirmed:** Allied heroes use soft collision: they gently separate when overlapping but can move through one another when necessary.
- **Confirmed:** Enemies occupy combat space and can surround heroes, but use sliding and separation behavior so they do not form immovable walls.
- **Confirmed:** Character collision must not allow allies or enemies to permanently trap a combatant against another character.
- **Confirmed:** Attacks, jumps, specials, and supers use short, selectively tuned input-buffer windows.
- **Confirmed:** The initial offensive input-buffer target is approximately 100–150 milliseconds and will be tuned by action category in the Combat Lab.
- **Confirmed:** Only one next action is queued; a newer valid input can replace the queued action.
- **Confirmed:** Movement input remains immediate rather than buffered.
- **Confirmed:** Exact-block and counter timing uses the real button-press timestamp, so offensive buffering cannot expand defensive timing windows.
- **Confirmed:** Dash cancellation follows the Tagged Combat Dash rules defined in Module 2: Basic Offense.
- **Deferred:** Detailed landing-recovery rules are decided in Module 4: Aerial Combat.
- **Confirmed:** Single-player supports seamless switching between keyboard and gamepad according to the device currently being used.
- **Confirmed:** Local co-op requires each player to join explicitly and assigns a keyboard or gamepad to that player.
- **Confirmed:** A newly connected controller never silently takes control from an existing player.
- **Confirmed:** Disconnecting an assigned controller pauses local gameplay and opens a reconnection or reassignment prompt.
- **Confirmed:** Local gameplay resumes after the controller reconnects or the player assigns another available device.

#### Module 1 validation goals

- **Proposed:** Keyboard and gamepad inputs produce the same abstract gameplay actions and combat opportunities.
- **Proposed:** Reassigned controls take effect correctly without changing combat behavior.
- **Proposed:** Eight-direction movement has normalized diagonal speed, preventing diagonal movement from being faster than horizontal or depth movement.
- **Proposed:** Direction double-taps and optionally bound dash inputs produce the same dash behavior without accidental invulnerability.
- **Proposed:** Neutral jumps, directional jumps, and limited aerial corrections behave consistently on keyboard, directional pad, and analog stick.
- **Proposed:** Manual Facing always honors explicit player direction; Assisted Facing selects predictably and never changes targets during an active combo.
- **Proposed:** Allied soft collision and enemy solid/sliding collision remain stable without jitter, permanent trapping, or immovable crowds.
- **Proposed:** Selective input buffering queues at most one valid action and never enlarges exact-block or counter timing windows.
- **Proposed:** Single-player device switching, co-op joining, controller disconnection, reconnection, and reassignment all preserve the correct player assignment.
- **Proposed:** The Combat Lab displays input history, active device assignment, facing or assisted target, movement state, speed, collision shapes, and the currently buffered action for diagnosis.

### 10.2 Module 2 — Basic Offense

- **Confirmed:** Normal attacks use a Structured Branching combo model.
- **Confirmed:** A dependable opening chain leads to controlled neutral or directional finishers.
- **Confirmed:** The normal Attack action uses `T` by default, with `W/A/S/D + T` selecting directional branches where the active hero's combo graph permits them.
- **Confirmed:** Structured Branching is preferred over a single fixed string because it offers meaningful player choice, and over unrestricted freeform cancellation because its routes and safety limits remain understandable and balanceable.
- **Confirmed:** Each branch has an explicit endpoint or transition, preventing the normal-attack graph from looping infinitely.
- **Confirmed:** Heroes use Shared Grammar with Unique Trees: every hero shares the normal-attack action and directional input language, while move names, timing, damage, reach, and branch properties remain hero-specific.
- **Confirmed:** Each founding hero's Representative Vertical Slice kit targets approximately **13–15 authored normal actions** across standing, aerial, ground, and dash combat. This is a production-scope budget rather than a rigid gameplay limit; a multi-hit technique counts as one authored action, while Specials, Ultimates, weapon actions, grabs, guards, counters, and Combo Breakers are counted separately.
- **Confirmed:** The 13–15-action target must provide a complete three-action beginner fallback, meaningful directional route and finisher choices, multiple aerial and ground options, a dash attack, and selected authored cancel intersections sufficient for Academy examples and player-discovered freestyle routes. Expanding to a launch-scale 18–24-action normal catalogue is deferred until the vertical-slice kits are validated.
- **Confirmed:** The founders use **Equal Depth with Role-Weighted Categories** for the Representative Vertical Slice. Each receives fifteen authored normal actions and the same essential combat coverage, while role-weighted category allocations reinforce character identity instead of forcing identical distributions.
- **Confirmed:** The Mountain Guardian's target distribution is seven standing actions, three aerial actions, three ground actions, one dash action, and one low attack. The River Scout's target distribution is seven standing actions, four aerial actions, two ground actions, one dash action, and one low attack. These counts define the authoring budget; they do not require equivalent move properties or mirrored combo graphs.
- **Confirmed:** The founders' seven-action standing trees use **Shared Directional Intent with Character-Owned Payoff**. Neutral `T` provides each hero's three-action fallback; `Forward + T` advances or carries pressure; `Backward + T` checks an approach or withdraws; and `Up + T` and `Down + T` attack while moving through the corresponding stage-depth direction. Move properties, reactions, routes, and positioning payoffs remain hero-specific rather than mirrored.
- **Confirmed:** The Mountain Guardian's working standing actions are **Mason's Touch**, **Brace Elbow**, and **Gatepost Hammer** for the neutral fallback; **Stone Shoulder** for forward guard pressure; **Anchor Hook** for a short retreating check; **High Bank Lift** for a depth-up launcher; and **Low Bank Sweep** for a depth-down knockdown setup.
- **Confirmed:** The River Scout's working standing actions are **Reed Tap**, **Turning Current**, and **Open Channel Kick** for the neutral fallback; **Rapids Step** for advancing carry; **Ebb Heel** for a retreating whiff punish; **Northbank Arc** for a depth-up pursuit starter; and **Southbank Slide** for a depth-down cross-through. All names remain provisional pending final character naming and localization.
- **Confirmed:** The founders' aerial suites use **Complete Coverage with Signature Flex**. After jumping with `Z`, airborne `T` supplies a fast air-to-air check, `Forward + T` supplies forward conversion, and `Up/Down + T` supplies stage-depth correction. Mountain expresses those shared needs through heavier knockback and rescue coverage, while River expresses them through pursuit, carry, and repositioning.
- **Confirmed:** The Mountain Guardian's working aerial actions are **Rafter Palm** (`Air T`), a fast air-to-air check; **Stonefall Knee** (`Forward + T`), a committed descending knockback; and **Cornerstone Sweep** (`Up/Down + T`), a broad depth-shift rescue arc.
- **Confirmed:** The River Scout's working aerial actions are **Reedwing Tap** (`Air T`), a fast pursuit check; **Current Chase** (`Forward + T`), an advancing carry kick; **Bank-Switch Arc** (`Up/Down + T`), a depth-shift pursuit; and **Ebbturn Heel** (`Backward + T`), a momentum brake with a retreating strike. Ebbturn Heel is an authored correction option rather than unrestricted aerial reversal or flight, and every aerial action remains subject to limited air correction, cancel tags, juggle budget, scaling, and protected recovery.
- **Confirmed:** The founders' ground and dash packages use **Shared Route with Role-Weighted Finishers**. Both heroes can perform one optional ground extension, proceed directly or indirectly to a ground finisher, and use a character-specific dash attack; neither dash action grants invulnerability, and every ground finisher closes the shared ground route.
- **Confirmed:** The Mountain Guardian's working ground actions are **Settling Palm** (`T` with Ground-Opportunity Focus), an extension that keeps the target close; **Lockgate Press** (`Forward + T` with Ground-Opportunity Focus), a brief submission finisher; and **Foundation Rise** (`Backward + T` with Ground-Opportunity Focus), a costly relaunch finisher. The working dash attack is **Rampart Charge** (`T` during a dash), a committed guard-pressure strike with a short push.
- **Confirmed:** The River Scout's working ground actions are **Skipping Current** (`T` with Ground-Opportunity Focus), an extension with lateral repositioning, and **Rising Eddy** (`Forward + T` with Ground-Opportunity Focus), a side-switching relaunch finisher. If relaunch is prohibited or its budget is exhausted, Rising Eddy resolves as an authored separating kick instead of failing or resetting the combo. The working dash attack is **Rapid Crossing** (`T` during a dash), a fast carry attack with an authored side switch.
- **Confirmed:** Low attacks are distinct from downed-target ground attacks. A low attack strikes a standing enemy with a lowered attack and hurtbox profile, may create a short stagger or trip, and remains blockable by normal Guard; it does not introduce a separate high/low guard system or grant invulnerability.
- **Confirmed:** Each founder receives one additional authored low attack, bringing both Representative Vertical Slice normal-action catalogues to fifteen. While a dash is already active, pressing `T + G` within the authored branch window selects the low attack instead of the ordinary `Dash + T` attack. In this specific chord `G` is an attack-branch modifier: it does not Guard, Exact Block, or spend Defense meter.
- **Confirmed:** Low Attack is also exposed as an abstract, optionally bindable action so a player may assign it to one keyboard key or gamepad button instead of using the default dash-state chord. Chord recognition, input priority, and remapping must prevent an attempted Low Attack from unpredictably becoming Guard or the ordinary dash attack.
- **Confirmed:** The Mountain Guardian's working low attack is **Faultline Reap**, a short, broad, committed sweep with stronger trip pressure. The River Scout's working low attack is **Undertow Slide**, a faster, longer, narrower slide that favors repositioning. The names remain provisional.
- **Confirmed:** A clean low hit may briefly stagger a standard enemy; a counter-hit or explicitly tagged finisher may trip the enemy and create an `opensGroundWindow` knockdown. Hit-stun decay, the existing ground-route state, and protected recovery prevent repeated low attacks from producing a stun or knockdown loop. Elites resist the reaction, and bosses normally receive damage and Resolve pressure without tripping unless already in an authored vulnerable state.
- **Confirmed:** The basic combo rhythm stays learnable when changing heroes, but mastery still requires learning the selected hero's distinct routes and tactical strengths.
- **Confirmed:** Directional normal-attack branches are fully character-specific. The same directional input may have a different move, tactical purpose, combat result, or valid route for each hero.
- **Confirmed:** Neutral `T` is a universal, dependable quick starter for every hero.
- **Confirmed:** Repeated neutral `T` completes a simple, safe basic fallback combo for every hero. Directional inputs replace or extend parts of that fallback through the active hero's unique combo tree.
- **Confirmed:** Learning a new hero includes learning that hero's directional moves and routes; each hero will therefore have an accessible move list.
- **Confirmed:** Normal-combo branch timing uses Route Choice plus Finisher: neutral `T` starts the combo, the second move can select a character-specific route, and the third move selects a character-specific finisher or completes the neutral fallback.
- **Confirmed:** Character combo graphs are deliberately sparse and authored. Heroes expose a limited set of intentional routes rather than every mathematical combination of directions.
- **Confirmed:** The three-move normal route is the accessible foundation, not a hard limit on a complete combat combo. Individual moves may contain multiple hits.
- **Confirmed:** Freestyle extensions use tagged, character-specific cancel windows. Selected starters, route moves, and finishers can transition into explicitly permitted aerial, ground, special, weapon, super, or cooperative follow-ups.
- **Confirmed:** Tagged cancel windows do not permit unrestricted move-to-move cancellation. Each transition is part of the hero's authored combo graph and remains subject to the universal anti-infinite rules.
- **Confirmed:** Cancel activation conditions are move-specific. Every cancelable move explicitly declares whether its transitions are permitted after hitting an enemy, contacting an enemy's guard, or whiffing.
- **Confirmed:** Offensive moves default to hit-confirmed cancels. Hit-or-guard cancels are reserved for selected pressure tools, while whiff cancels are rare and primarily support deliberate mobility or stance transitions.
- **Confirmed:** An attack cancel is activated by entering the permitted next move during the current move's cancel window. It does not use the Guard action.
- **Confirmed:** The `G` action remains dedicated to the defensive system except when it is used as the explicitly authored modifier in the founders' dash-state Low Attack chord. The term Guard Cancel is reserved for a separate defensive mechanic and is not part of the current design.
- **Confirmed:** Player-facing explanations use Attack Cancel Window or Combo Transition to distinguish offensive cancels from guarding.
- **Confirmed:** Cancel conditions and valid destinations are shown in the hero move list and Academy lessons.
- **Confirmed:** Attack Cancel Window timing uses Category-Tuned plus Pre-Buffer. Each transition category owns its own early-input buffer and post-contact cancel window instead of inheriting one universal duration.
- **Confirmed:** One valid follow-up attack may be queued during a category's early-input buffer, consistent with the combat system's one-action input queue.
- **Confirmed:** The early buffer applies to offensive combo transitions only. It does not widen the timing windows for exact block, counterattack, or other defensive reactions.
- **Confirmed:** Cancel timing must remain dependable across supported keyboard and gamepad input and normal browser-performance variation.
- **Provisional:** The Combat Lab's special-cancel example accepts input from 120 ms before contact through 170 ms after contact. These are tuning targets for the example category, not final values or universal values for every cancel category.
- **Confirmed:** Invalid cancel inputs use Reason-Aware Handling. A valid linked follow-up entered inside its approved early buffer may occupy the one-action queue.
- **Confirmed:** Inputs made before the early buffer, after the cancel window, toward an unlinked destination, or without satisfying the move's hit, guard, or whiff condition are discarded instead of becoming delayed standalone attacks.
- **Confirmed:** The Combat Lab and Academy show a specific rejection reason for invalid cancels. Normal combat communicates rejection through subtle audiovisual feedback without diagnostic text interrupting play.
- **Confirmed:** An invalid cancel never replaces an already queued valid action.
- **Confirmed:** Freestyle combo extensions use Destination Pays plus Premium Cancels. Ordinary tagged transitions into normal, aerial, ground, weapon, and standard-special follow-ups add no cancel surcharge.
- **Confirmed:** A destination move still pays its own defined resource cost. This keeps move costs data-driven and prevents the cancel system from silently making an otherwise costly move free.
- **Confirmed:** Rare, unusually powerful recovery-skipping transitions are tagged as premium cancels and consume attack meter. The Combat Lab uses a provisional cost of 25% attack meter for this category.
- **Confirmed:** Supers remain a destination that requires and consumes a full attack meter; they do not add a second cancel surcharge.
- **Confirmed:** Defense meter never pays for offensive cancel extensions. It remains reserved for guarding and combo breakers.
- **Confirmed:** Cancel costs are committed atomically when a valid transition actually begins. Invalid routes, unmet contact conditions, closed timing windows, and insufficient-meter attempts consume nothing.
- **Confirmed:** The existing attack meter funds premium cancels; the system does not introduce another combat resource.
- **Confirmed:** Dash cancellation uses the Tagged Combat Dash model. A neutral dash may lead into a character-specific dash attack without consuming the combo's dash bridge.
- **Confirmed:** Only explicitly tagged attacks may cancel into a dash. Each tag declares whether the transition is valid after a hit, contact with an enemy's guard, or a whiff; whiff-enabled dash cancels are rare mobility tools.
- **Confirmed:** An attack-to-dash transition skips attack recovery and therefore uses the premium-cancel attack-meter cost. The Combat Lab provisionally charges 25% attack meter.
- **Confirmed:** A continuous combo may use only one attack-to-dash bridge. The allowance resets when that combo ends and combat returns to neutral, preventing attack-to-dash loops without adding another visible resource meter.
- **Confirmed:** A dash reached through the bridge may enter only authored, character-specific follow-ups in the active hero's combo graph.
- **Confirmed:** Insufficient meter, an untagged move, an invalid contact condition, or an already-spent dash bridge rejects the transition, spends no meter, and does not queue a delayed dash.
- **Confirmed:** Neither a neutral dash nor a dash cancel grants invulnerability; Guard and the defensive system retain that role.
- **Confirmed:** Continuous combos use Target Context plus Tagged Continuations. Every defending combatant owns an independent combo context, covering heroes, ordinary enemies, and bosses under the same lifecycle rules.
- **Confirmed:** A combo context begins with the first confirmed hit against that target and continues while the target remains non-actionable through hit-stun, juggle, or another valid combat state.
- **Confirmed:** Explicit move-data tags may bridge an approved state seam for a ground follow-up, dash chase, or cooperative handoff. A universal grace timer does not preserve a combo after the target has regained actionable control.
- **Confirmed:** A combo ends when the target becomes actionable without a valid continuation, an authored continuation window expires, protected recovery begins, a hero activates a combo breaker, or a boss activates its resolve burst.
- **Confirmed:** Changing targets creates a separate combo context. In co-op, both heroes contribute to the same context when attacking the same target, while individual damage and participation remain trackable.
- **Confirmed:** Closing a combo context resets its dash-bridge allowance, breaker allowance, hit-stun decay, damage scaling, juggle count, knockdown or ground-follow-up limits, and other per-combo state.
- **Confirmed:** Academy recording and combo validation use the same lifecycle boundary. Ending a target's context closes the current recipe sequence; a later hit begins a new sequence.
- **Confirmed:** Hit-stun decay uses a Move-Weighted Stun Budget stored inside each target's combo context. It is an internal combat value rather than a new player-facing resource meter.
- **Confirmed:** Every target context begins with the same provisional 100-point hit-stun budget. Each offensive action spends an authored `stunDecayCost`, allowing light attacks, launchers, specials, ground follow-ups, weapons, and cooperative attacks to carry appropriate weights.
- **Confirmed:** A multi-hit move normally pays one authored decay cost for the complete offensive action rather than one cost per visual hit. Separately cancellable phases may declare separate costs when they function as distinct actions.
- **Provisional:** Above 70 remaining budget, moves retain 100% of authored hit-stun; from 31 through 70 they apply 75%; from 1 through 30 they apply 50%.
- **Confirmed:** When an action exhausts the budget, that action still connects and deals damage, but its concluding reaction forces safe separation appropriate to the target state, such as grounded soft knockback, air recovery, or protected get-up. Further offensive cancels close.
- **Confirmed:** Ending the target's combo context restores the full hit-stun budget. The algorithm applies to heroes, enemies, weapons, bosses, and cooperative attacks; boss resolve and hero combo breakers remain additional independent escape systems.
- **Confirmed:** Hit-stun decay changes control duration, not damage. Damage scaling is refined separately so the two balance effects can be evaluated independently.
- **Confirmed:** Juggle limits use a Move-Weighted Juggle Budget plus State Quotas stored inside each target-owned combo context. The budget is an internal control value rather than another player-facing meter.
- **Confirmed:** Every target context provisionally begins with 100 juggle points. Actions spend an authored `juggleCost` when they launch, hold, redirect, or relaunch an airborne target; light aerial continuations, finishers, relaunchers, wall rebounds, and ground bounces may therefore carry different costs.
- **Confirmed:** A multi-hit aerial move normally pays one juggle cost for the complete offensive action rather than one cost per visual hit. Separately cancellable phases may declare separate costs when they behave as distinct actions.
- **Provisional:** From 61 through 100 remaining juggle points, moves retain their full authored lift and trajectory; from 31 through 60 they produce reduced lift and air hold; from 1 through 30 they produce minimal lift with stronger downward momentum.
- **Confirmed:** When an action exhausts the juggle budget, that action still connects and deals damage, but it concludes with protected landing or air recovery. Further aerial cancels and cooperative handoffs close, and the target cannot be relaunched before protected recovery ends.
- **Provisional:** A target combo permits one ordinary wall rebound and one ordinary ground bounce. The same bounce state cannot be alternated repeatedly; authored Super or team-finisher exceptions conclude the combo rather than refreshing it.
- **Confirmed:** Cooperative attackers share the defending target's juggle budget and state quotas, so changing attackers never refreshes airborne control. Changing targets uses the other target's independent context.
- **Confirmed:** Heroes, ordinary enemies, weapons, and bosses use the same juggle-control algorithm. Large enemies and bosses may reject launch reactions except during authored vulnerable states.
- **Confirmed:** Juggle budget, hit-stun budget, and damage scaling remain independent. A move may separately define damage proration, control-duration cost, and airborne-control cost.
