- **Confirmed:** Damage scaling uses Move-Level Proration plus a Damage Floor inside each target-owned combo context. A new target context begins at 100% damage scale, and the scale resets only when that context ends.
- **Confirmed:** The opening move may apply authored starter proration to later actions. Fast or safe starters can preserve less future damage than riskier punish, counter, or commitment-heavy starters.
- **Confirmed:** Each offensive action carries an authored `damageScaleCost` that reduces the scale applied to the next action, supporting character-specific normals, aerials, ground attacks, specials, weapons, enemy attacks, boss attacks, and cooperative moves under one algorithm.
- **Confirmed:** A multi-hit move locks one damage scale for its complete authored move damage and distributes that scaled total across its visual hits. Visual hit count alone does not repeatedly lower the scale.
- **Provisional:** Ordinary moves have a global 30% damage floor. Supers and cinematic cooperative finishers may declare their own minimum floors, but they never ignore damage scaling entirely.
- **Confirmed:** Cooperative attackers share the defending target's damage scale, so handing a combo between heroes or alternating attackers cannot reset scaling. Changing targets uses the other target's independent combo context.
- **Confirmed:** Damage is calculated from the move-level scaled total to avoid per-hit rounding drift. Damage scaling affects life damage only; hit-stun decay, meter gain, juggle limits, and continuation validity remain independent systems.
- **Confirmed:** Difficulty may tune health, defense, behavior, and authored move data, but does not silently disable the universal damage-scaling algorithm.
- **Unresolved:** Exact starter-proration values, per-action scale costs, ordinary and special damage floors, and final rounding rules remain tuning values. The Move-Level Proration plus Damage Floor model itself is confirmed.
- **Unresolved:** Final juggle-budget size, per-action costs, reaction bands, bounce quotas, and target-specific launch eligibility remain tuning values. The Move-Weighted Juggle Budget plus State Quotas model itself is confirmed.
- **Unresolved:** Final budget size, per-move decay costs, multiplier thresholds, and state-specific forced-separation reactions remain tuning values. The Move-Weighted Stun Budget model itself is confirmed.
- **Unresolved:** Exact per-category cancel timings, the final premium-cancel cost, and any destination-specific move costs. These values will be tuned per hero and move without changing the confirmed cost model.

### 10.3 Player Academy

- **Confirmed:** A player-facing Academy mode will teach the combat system separately from the developer-oriented Combat Lab.
- **Confirmed:** The Academy uses a **Seven-Course Progressive Academy** from basic operation to advanced mastery: Orientation and Controls; Combat Foundations; Defense and Survival; Conversion and Combo Grammar; Resources, Equipment, and Environments; Hero and Co-op Specialization; and Mastery and Freestyle. The existing Foundation, Conversion, Resource, and Freestyle cancel-grammar tiers remain nested inside this broader curriculum rather than being replaced.
- **Confirmed:** Every authored lesson follows **Demonstration -> Guided Practice -> Independent Trial -> Optional Mastery Goal**. Failure has no penalty and supports instant retry. Slow motion, input history, timing indicators, current bindings, and accessibility assists remain available without altering ordinary combat rules.
- **Confirmed:** Basic lessons are available from first launch. Hero courses unlock with their heroes; mounts, advanced weapons, environments, enemy specialists, and other spoiler-sensitive lessons unlock only after their content is introduced. Every revealed technique has a free trial. An optional placement assessment may open higher course tiers for experienced players but grants no skipped challenge rewards, technique ownership, or false completion.
- **Confirmed:** Story progression never requires Academy completion. Cheat Practice remains separate and awards no Credits, achievements, Hero Mastery, challenge completion, or technique ownership.
- **Confirmed:** Academy progression uses an **Open Curriculum with Four Learning States**: Available, Practiced after guided work, Certified after the independent trial, and Mastered after the optional mastery goal. Placement assessment may grant **Access Granted** without falsely assigning completion. Free Practice is always available; prerequisites exist only for genuine mechanical dependencies; hero and spoiler-sensitive branches unlock with their content; new branches never reset old progress; each persistent profile records its own state; Guests retain none; and relevant results, Guide, Move List, and Store surfaces may deep-link to lessons.
- **Confirmed:** Academy content includes universal fundamentals, hero-specific courses, authored example combos, and free practice for discovering freestyle routes.
- **Confirmed:** Cancel-map teaching uses **Transparent Map with Progressive Lessons**. Every legal destination, contact condition, resource requirement, and relevant target-state condition is available in the move library and Combat Lab; mechanical links are not hidden to manufacture discovery.
- **Confirmed:** Academy progression teaches the cancel grammar in four layers: **Foundation** for the safe normal route, **Conversion** for launch, aerial, low, and ground continuations, **Resource** for Specials, premium dash bridges, and Ultimates, and **Freestyle** for composing and recording player-authored recipes from the same visible graph.
- **Confirmed:** The Academy teaches representative routes rather than enumerating every mathematical combination. Each founder receives at least one beginner fallback, one directional-branch lesson, one aerial conversion, one low-to-ground conversion, one Special or premium-dash resource route, one Ultimate-finisher route, and shared cooperative launch, handoff, and finisher practice.
- **Confirmed:** Representative Mountain Guardian recipes include **Mason's Touch → Brace Elbow → Gatepost Hammer**, **High Bank Lift → jump chase → Rafter Palm → Stonefall Knee**, and **Faultline Reap counter-trip → Settling Palm → Lockgate Press or Foundation Rise**.
- **Confirmed:** Representative River Scout recipes include **Reed Tap → Turning Current → Open Channel Kick**, **Northbank Arc → jump chase → Reedwing Tap → Current Chase**, and **Undertow Slide counter-trip → Skipping Current → Rising Eddy**.
- **Confirmed:** Player discovery means finding useful compositions, timings, positioning, and cooperative applications within documented rules. A discovered legal route may be named, saved, replayed, and practiced in My Combos without becoming a hidden prerequisite for core progression.
- **Confirmed:** Beginners can learn basic fallback and recommended combo chains before progressing to tagged cancel windows and freestyle discovery.
- **Confirmed:** Players can record, name, save, replay, practice, and delete their discovered combo chains in a My Combos area.
- **Confirmed:** The first recording system stores replayable combo recipes rather than gameplay video.
- **Confirmed:** A saved recipe uses abstract gameplay actions instead of physical keyboard keys or gamepad buttons, preserving it across control remapping.
- **Confirmed:** A saved recipe can include the hero, weapon, move sequence, input timing, meter requirements, starting enemy state, hit count, damage, and relevant combat-state transitions.
- **Confirmed:** Combat rules, move lists, Academy lessons, combo validation, demonstration playback, and saved recipes draw from the same character move and cancel data.
- **Confirmed:** The village elder can introduce and explain Academy lessons through localized dialogue.
- **Confirmed:** Academy Free Practice and a dedicated Cheat Practice filter may use any revealed cheat or preset. My Combos records the canonical cheat configuration when a recipe depends on modified meter, weapon, damage, or reaction rules.
- **Confirmed:** Authored Academy lessons, exams, technique-acquisition challenges, and mastery trials use their declared rules and do not accept arbitrary cheats. Cheat Practice awards no Credits, Hero Mastery, achievements, or technique ownership.
- **Deferred:** Full Academy implementation begins after the core Combat Lab mechanics are stable.
- **Confirmed:** Guide and Academy follow a **Reference versus Practice Contract**. Guide/How to Play is the searchable localized text, diagram, control-card, and example reference available from the Village and pause menus; it has no reading progress requirement or rewards. Academy is the separate interactive destination for demonstrations, drills, trials, mastery goals, and free practice; its declared certification and mastery activities may grant bounded rewards.
- **Confirmed:** Stable controls, move properties, resource costs, cancel conditions, and defense rules come from one authoritative data source. Guide explains them, Academy executes them, and both may deep-link to each other and the Move List. Guide remains usable without video, audio, precision timing, or pointer; Academy provides slow motion, input history, timing help, captions, and instant retry. Guide never simulates combat, Academy never becomes the only source of an essential explanation, and neither gates Story access.
- **Confirmed:** Curated Missions experiment with **Event-Tagged Replay Highlights**. A presentation-only observer records bounded candidate frame IDs and combat facts from existing deterministic events; it captures no continuous screenshots and changes no simulation state. At results, candidates are ranked, diversified, and limited to three. Deterministic replay reconstructs clean screenshots one at a time, while localized titles, reasons, attribution, and facts remain separate UI text.
- **Confirmed:** Highlight cards support View Replay Moment, Save Screenshot, and Pin to Museum Highlights. They may identify Player 1, Player 2, or a Team Highlight, merge nearby moments, avoid duplicate categories, remain local unless explicitly exported, provide screen-reader text, and fall back to text-only presentation when rendering is unavailable. Highlights grant no score, Credits, rank, Mastery, or achievements during the experiment and never affect AI, damage, timing, randomness, synchronization, or Combat Constitution legality.
- **Confirmed:** Curated Mission highlight capture uses **Default-On Bounded Highlight Capture**. New profiles default to On; Settings -> Presentation and every Curated Mission selection screen provide a persistent override. Local-Wi-Fi devices choose independently, while same-device co-op shares the display setting. Enabled collection retains at most 24 compact event candidates with fixed processing cost and no live image capture; disabled collection runs no highlight-specific observer or renderer.
- **Confirmed:** Results appear and save immediately before screenshots reconstruct one at a time. Generation is skippable, may reduce image resolution, and falls back to localized text without blocking results. Capability preflight may recommend Off but cannot silently disable a supported feature. Sustained performance pressure stops optional collection gracefully. Highlight state remains outside deterministic simulation and network messages, and its setting never changes score, rewards, eligibility, replay truth, or adaptive gameplay behavior.
- **Confirmed:** Event-Tagged Replay Highlights use six hero-normalized families: **Perfect Combo / 完美连段 / Perfekte Kombo**; **Two Threats One Move / 一石二鸟 / Zwei Gegner ein Zug**; Guardian Intervention; Defensive Mastery; Team Harmony; and Environmental Ingenuity. Perfect Combo requires one uninterrupted target context, at least three distinct actions, at least two route families, no rejected cancel or repeated-action spam, no damage received, an intentional legal conclusion, and a hero-normalized impact/efficiency benchmark. Two Threats One Move requires one authored action to create a meaningful legal outcome on at least two active hostile targets; props, allies, protected targets, invulnerable contacts, and repeated hits on one target do not count.
- **Confirmed:** Highlight selection permits at most one result per family and three total, merges or chooses between nearby moments, assigns one strongest label to a multi-qualifying moment, and ranks tactical impact, difficulty, risk, resource efficiency, rarity, and objective protection. Hero normalization prevents multi-hit or high-damage kits from dominating. Assisted or Cheat-Practice moments show their ruleset label. Localized explanations come from authored templates populated with verified combat facts; exact benchmarks remain validated tuning data.
- **Confirmed:** Highlight rules use **Layered Highlight Disclosure**. Guide owns the complete localized Automatic Highlights article, including all six families, selection rules, normalization, co-op attribution, ruleset labels, performance, privacy, storage, replay, Museum, and no-reward boundary. Curated Mission selection shows the On/Off toggle, a concise explanation, six localized category icons, and a lossless View Highlight Criteria link. Result cards provide an expandable Why Was This Selected explanation and criterion link; pinned Museum cards retain the reason and link. Every surface consumes the same localization keys and criterion data. Academy contains no required highlight lesson.
- **Confirmed:** Highlight images use **Ephemeral by Default, Pin to Keep** storage. Result-screen stills are temporary; the Run Archive retains only the compact candidate metadata, verified facts, and compatible replay reference needed to reconstruct them. Leaving results discards an unpinned image, while **Pin to Museum Highlights** or explicit export preserves it. Pinned images use a compressed 1280x720 internal representation; clean screenshot export uses PNG, and localized highlight-card export may include the authored title and reason outside the clean image.
- **Confirmed:** Each persistent profile initially supports 50 pinned highlight images. The game warns before the budget is reached and provides filtering, renaming, exporting, unpinning, and confirmed deletion without silently removing a pin. Identical images are deduplicated by content hash. A profile backup may include pinned images or use a metadata-only option; deleting or unpinning an image never deletes the underlying run record. Each persistent co-op player may pin a shared moment to their own profile, same-device copies reuse deduplicated storage, local-Wi-Fi players manage their own device copies, and Guests may export but cannot create persistent pins.
- **Confirmed:** Curated Mission replays use **Tiered Replay Retention with Pin Guarantee**. Compact Run Archive metadata, score facts, and localized Highlight explanations remain for every result. Full deterministic capsules are retained for the personal best in each comparable Curated category, the five most recent Curated runs per profile, every manually pinned replay, and every run with a Highlight pinned to Museum Highlights. A Museum Highlight pin therefore guarantees **View Replay Moment** while its replay remains version-compatible.
- **Confirmed:** Older unpinned, non-best Curated capsules may compact under storage pressure. Their facts and explanations remain with a clear **Replay No Longer Retained** state, while playback and still reconstruction become unavailable. Incompatible updates preserve existing pinned images, reasons, facts, and Legacy status but never simulate them inaccurately. Unpinning makes a capsule eligible for normal compaction only when no other retention rule protects it. Profile backup offers full-capsule and metadata-only forms.
- **Confirmed:** Automatic Highlights launch behind a **Curated-Only Validation Gate**. They remain limited to the four Curated Missions until every mission proves identical combat hashes and outcomes with capture enabled or disabled; no highlight traffic in local-Wi-Fi synchronization; the fixed 24-candidate bound and validated negligible frame-time cost; immediate result saving and cancelable reconstruction; reliable authored-replay detection with human quality review; complete English, Simplified Chinese, and German explanations; keyboard, gamepad, screen-reader, backup, export, and storage-limit operation; and zero influence on score, rewards, rank, or achievements.
- **Confirmed:** Automatic Highlights upload no analytics or images by default. Diagnostic or feedback export is an explicit player action. Capture may be disabled for affected content without losing runs, metadata, exports, or existing Museum pins. Passing the Curated Mission gate does not automatically enable highlights elsewhere: any later expansion to Random Operations, Chapter Challenge, Story, or another mode requires a separately approved scope and validation decision.

## 11. Provisional Phase 3 roadmap

- **Confirmed:** Phase 3 module boundaries are defined now, while detailed refinement waits until the Phase 2 combat, enemy, co-op, and browser constraints are stable.
- **Planned — Module 14: Academy and Move Library.** Tutorials, hero courses, combo trials, free practice, move lists, and combo-recipe recording and replay.
- **Planned — Module 15: Difficulty, Progression, and Saves.** Difficulty behavior, unlocks, completion tracking, save data, and progression persistence.
- **Planned — Module 16: Game Shell, Guide, Cheats, and Accessibility.** Main menu, game-mode selection, hero selection, Guide/How to Play, cheat configuration, settings, control remapping, pause, HUD, subtitles, and accessibility options.
  - **Confirmed:** The game shell uses a **Hybrid Village Command Menu**: an illustrated or lightly animated village presentation with concise focus-based navigation rather than a physically explorable hub.
  - **Confirmed:** The primary destinations are Continue, Play, Academy, Village Store, Collection and Records, Guide, and Settings. If an eligible checkpoint exists, Continue receives default focus; otherwise Play receives default focus.
  - **Confirmed:** Play follows `mode → participating profiles and assigned controllers → stage and difficulty → heroes → intro cinematic → elder briefing and loadout → readiness → gameplay`.
  - **Confirmed:** Solo, same-device co-op, and local-Wi-Fi setup all enter through Play. Local-Wi-Fi continuation always returns through its lobby and compatibility/readiness checks rather than automatically resuming a network session.
  - **Confirmed:** Keyboard, gamepad, and pointer can operate every game-shell screen, and a mouse is never required. The village presentation supplies atmosphere without forcing repeated avatar travel between menu functions.
  - **Confirmed:** A first-launch setup selects language, subtitle defaults, essential accessibility preferences, and the initial input device before entering the Village Command Menu. Secondary statistics and configuration remain inside their relevant destinations instead of crowding the main menu.
  - **Confirmed:** The combat HUD uses **Adaptive Corner Clusters with Contextual Information**. Player 1's persistent Health, Defense, Attack Bar, portrait, lives or continues, and essential status indicators occupy the upper-left; Player 2 receives a mirrored upper-right cluster in co-op, which disappears entirely in solo play.
  - **Confirmed:** Boss Health and Resolve appear centrally only during the relevant boss encounter. Ordinary enemy information remains target-local or transient rather than creating permanent additional HUD bars.
  - **Confirmed:** Combo count, damage, Exact Block, Counter, co-op handoff, rescue, warnings, and similar combat feedback appear temporarily near the relevant player's side and clear after their authored display window.
  - **Confirmed:** Interaction prompts use the lower edge only while actionable. Objectives appear at stage start, on update, or when explicitly recalled instead of occupying a permanent expanded panel.
  - **Confirmed:** When an Attack Bar becomes full, the HUD briefly exposes the equipped directional Ultimate choices before reducing to a compact ready indicator. Move lists and full loadout explanations remain in briefing, pause, and Academy surfaces.
  - **Confirmed:** HUD accessibility settings can enlarge bars, labels, prompts, subtitles, contrast, and warning presentation without changing combat simulation, resource values, or timing.
  - **Confirmed:** Accessibility uses **Layered Accessibility with Declared Assisted Rulesets**. Presentation and control aids that do not change simulation remain available in every mode without altering score categories or progression labels.
  - **Confirmed:** Universal aids include full keyboard and gamepad remapping, per-player facing preference, appropriate hold/toggle alternatives, subtitle sizing and backgrounds, speaker labels, HUD scaling, high contrast, color-plus-symbol indicators, readable-font options, reduced flashing, reduced camera shake and particles, independent audio-channel levels, visual equivalents for essential sound cues, and configurable controller vibration.
  - **Confirmed:** A setting that materially changes simulation or validated challenge timing creates an explicitly marked Assisted Ruleset. Examples may include wider selected response windows, reduced repeated-input requirements, additional warning cues, bounded combo-input assistance, and solo/Academy game-speed reduction; full automatic combat is not the default accessibility model.
  - **Confirmed:** Assisted play retains story progress, checkpoints, purchases, ordinary Credit earnings, and Hero Mastery. Assisted results are stored separately, while competitive Standard/Open records and achievements specifically testing original timing use the validated unassisted rules.
  - **Confirmed:** If either co-op participant enables a simulation-changing assist, the team result is marked Assisted. Local-Wi-Fi participants see and accept the declared assist configuration before readying, and solo game-speed reduction is unavailable during synchronized co-op.
  - **Confirmed:** Presentation and device-local control preferences may differ per player without marking a run Assisted. Accessibility state is saved per persistent profile and included in portable profile backups.
  - **Confirmed:** Pausing uses a **Focused Menu with a Contextual Detail Pane**. Resume receives default focus; selecting another short primary entry opens only its relevant information and controls rather than displaying a full dashboard.
  - **Confirmed:** Pause entries include Resume, Move List, How to Play, Controls, Accessibility and Audio, Current Objective, Restart from Checkpoint, and Return to Village. The Move List reflects the current hero, combat style, equipped Specials and Ultimates, and active weapon.
  - **Confirmed:** Difficulty, loadout, score category, and other stage rules remain visible but locked during an active stage. Non-simulation audio, subtitle, display, HUD, vibration, accessibility, and control settings may be changed safely.
  - **Confirmed:** In solo and Academy, enabling a simulation-changing assist while paused requires confirmation and marks the current result Assisted from that point. Co-op participants cannot change simulation-changing assists after stage readiness; those changes wait for the next eligible briefing or preparation boundary.
  - **Confirmed:** Restart from Checkpoint and Return to Village require explicit confirmation. In co-op, both participating players must agree before either operation changes the shared run.
  - **Confirmed:** Local-Wi-Fi pause displays each participant's readiness. Resume begins only after every participant confirms and the synchronized countdown completes; stale gameplay inputs are cleared before simulation resumes.
  - **Confirmed:** Hero selection uses **Split Independent Selection with Unique Heroes**. Each player receives a dedicated selection and loadout panel, and both players may browse the roster simultaneously.
  - **Confirmed:** Every hero represents one unique person from the village and therefore may be selected by only one participant in a run. Duplicate-hero teams are unavailable in solo, same-device co-op, local-Wi-Fi co-op, and Academy activities.
  - **Confirmed:** Both players may inspect the same unclaimed hero. Confirming a hero reserves that character for the confirming player; the other panel immediately presents the hero as selected by that player and requires a different choice. Canceling or changing the selection releases the reservation.
  - **Confirmed:** Each participant configures that hero's profile-owned outfit, combat style, and loadout independently, then confirms readiness. Changing the selected hero or any stage-relevant loadout choice clears only that participant's ready state.
  - **Confirmed:** The unique-hero rule preserves canonical identity, dialogue, relationships, progression, co-op interactions, and story continuity. Future roster additions increase co-op team variety without changing this rule.
  - **Confirmed:** Control remapping uses a **Guided Action List with a Live Tester**. Actions are grouped by context and presented through keyboard/gamepad-navigable player and device tabs rather than requiring pointer interaction with a device illustration.
  - **Confirmed:** Remapping listens for the player's next actual input and displays the character or control the active layout reports. On German QWERTZ, pressing the printed `Z` key therefore appears as `Z`; the interface never substitutes a US-layout label merely because of the key's physical browser code.
  - **Confirmed:** Mappings are saved per persistent profile and device type. Temporary Guest mappings remain session-local, and an assigned device cannot silently overwrite another player's controls.
  - **Confirmed:** A same-context binding conflict offers explicit Replace, Swap, or Cancel choices. Bindings in non-overlapping contexts may coexist, while protected menu navigation and recovery controls ensure that a player cannot leave the interface unusable.
  - **Confirmed:** Players may apply presets, reset one action group or an entire device map, and use a non-gameplay live tester for directions, buttons, chords, and analog response before accepting changes. Invalid or incomplete mappings remain unapplied with a localized explanation.
  - **Confirmed:** Player, profile, controller, and network assembly uses an **Adaptive Readiness Lobby**. The same player-card structure serves every mode while revealing only the preparation and validation information required by that mode.
  - **Confirmed:** Solo play restores the last eligible persistent profile and follows the currently active assigned device, allowing an immediate continuation while keeping profile and device changes available before the stage begins.
  - **Confirmed:** In same-device co-op, Player 2 explicitly joins by pressing an input on an available source, then chooses a persistent local profile or temporary Guest. One physical input source belongs to only one player; because browsers cannot reliably distinguish separate physical keyboards, all keyboards on the device count as one keyboard source while individual gamepads remain separately assignable.
  - **Confirmed:** In local-Wi-Fi co-op, each participant selects a profile and controller on their own device. The adaptive lobby replaces local join instructions with pairing, compatibility, connection-quality, content-validation, and remote-readiness status.
  - **Confirmed:** Each participant owns an independent ready state. Changing a personal profile, assigned controller, hero, or stage-relevant loadout clears only that participant's readiness; changing a shared session rule such as stage or difficulty clears every participant's readiness.
  - **Confirmed:** Newly connected devices never take ownership silently. Disconnection identifies the affected player and offers reconnection or explicit reassignment without altering the other participant's profile or device assignment.
  - **Confirmed:** Settings uses a **Focused Category Rail with a Detail Pane**. Persistent categories provide fast context-preserving navigation, while the focused setting receives its explanation, control, current value, consequences, and any available preview without exposing every option at once.
  - **Confirmed:** Initial categories are Gameplay, Controls, Accessibility, Audio, Display, Language, and Profile Data. The layout remains fully operable with keyboard, gamepad, or pointer and collapses the rail into an equivalent compact category selector when horizontal space is limited.
  - **Confirmed:** Every setting displays an ownership or consequence scope such as Profile, Device, Session, or Assisted. Shared co-op restrictions and temporarily unavailable options remain visible with a localized explanation of when and where they may be changed.
  - **Confirmed:** Ordinary reversible presentation, audio, subtitle, HUD, and preference changes preview and apply immediately. A display change that could make the interface difficult to use receives a visible automatic-revert countdown unless the player confirms it.
  - **Confirmed:** Enabling a simulation-changing Assisted option requires explicit confirmation and explains its record-category effect before applying. Profile import, replacement, and other consequential data operations use their previously defined previews and confirmations rather than behaving like ordinary toggles.
  - **Confirmed:** Each category offers a scoped reset with confirmation and a changed-state summary. Controls opens the approved guided remapping surface, while audiovisual and accessibility settings provide representative previews where practical.
  - **Confirmed:** Guide/How to Play is a localized text-reference system distinct from Academy's learning-by-doing activities. Its chapters cover getting started, controls/remapping, movement/facing, standing/aerial/low/ground combat, defense, combos/cancels/scaling, Specials/Ultimates/loadouts, grabs/weapons/items, solo/co-op, difficulty/enemies, progression/store/records, accessibility/Assisted rules, Academy/My Combos, Elder's Secrets, and a glossary.
  - **Confirmed:** Guide articles display current abstract-action bindings instead of hard-coded keyboard or controller labels. Stable mechanical facts are generated from the same rules and action data used by Move Lists and Academy, while authored explanations use English, Simplified Chinese, and German localization keys with the approved English fallback behavior.
  - **Confirmed:** All beginner-oriented codes and four presets are openly documented from first launch under **Guide → Elder's Secrets**. No story clear, defeat count, achievement, Credit payment, or Academy completion is required; future novelty codes may be secret, but assistance codes may not be progression-gated.
  - **Confirmed:** Cheats use stable language-independent ASCII identifiers. Entry is case-insensitive; surrounding whitespace is ignored; spaces, hyphens, and underscores inside a token are equivalent; misspellings are never guessed. Unknown, unavailable, conflicting, superseded, migrated, or unsupported codes produce a localized explanation and activate nothing silently.
  - **Confirmed:** Keyboard users may type a code, but typed entry is optional flavor rather than an access requirement. Keyboard, gamepad, and pointer users can select localized activation cards directly. Cards show code, scope, exact effect, conflicts, preset membership, and reward consequences before Add, Remove, Academy Preview, or Apply actions.
  - **Confirmed:** Gamepad operation uses remappable abstract menu actions—Confirm, Back, Previous Category, and Next Category—and never requires an on-screen keyboard. Presets appear before individually grouped Survival, Defense, Offense, Equipment, and Session cards.
- **Confirmed:** The hero-scoped codes are `SOFTERBLOWS` (50% incoming Health damage), `SAFEHEART` (no Health loss while reactions remain), `STEADYFEET` (knockdowns become bounded standing stumbles), `IRONGUARD` (no Defense consumption from blocks or Combo Breakers), `BRIGHTSPIRIT` (Attack Bar remains full), `STRONGHANDS` (double Health and Resolve damage), `TRUSTYTOOLS` (no weapon integrity loss), and `STEADYRIDE` (mounted Stability does not decrease while readable mounted reactions remain). `SAFEHEART` supersedes `SOFTERBLOWS`.
- **Confirmed:** The session-scoped codes are `QUIETCROWD` (Easy-level commitment and pressure limits while retaining the selected profile's enemies, variants, moves, formations, bosses, and story), `FULLPANTRY` (additional deterministic food from eligible containers without replacing weapons/objectives), `HOMECOMING` (defeat returns to the latest valid checkpoint, or restarts the stage if none exists), and `SAFEPASSAGE` (mandatory protected people and objects cannot cross into a completion-blocking failed, dead, Lost, or permanently destroyed state).
- **Confirmed:** `SAFEPASSAGE` allows a protected target to reach its visible Critical condition and preserves damage, reactions, warnings, interception pressure, escort movement, routes, evidence identification, escape-network tasks, enemies, bosses, and required player actions. It never completes an objective automatically. Optional preservation assets remain vulnerable unless their loss would block mandatory completion. In Random Operations Safeguard, the target cannot progress from Critical to Compromised or Lost.
- **Confirmed:** The initial presets are `FIRSTSTEPS` = `SOFTERBLOWS` + `IRONGUARD` + `STEADYRIDE` + `HOMECOMING`; `CALMBATTLE` = `SOFTERBLOWS` + `QUIETCROWD` + `FULLPANTRY` + `STEADYRIDE` + `HOMECOMING`; `PEACEFULPATH` = `SAFEHEART` + `IRONGUARD` + `STEADYRIDE` + `SAFEPASSAGE` + `HOMECOMING`; and `UNSTOPPABLE` = `SAFEHEART` + `STEADYFEET` + `IRONGUARD` + `BRIGHTSPIRIT` + `STRONGHANDS` + `TRUSTYTOOLS` + `STEADYRIDE` + `SAFEPASSAGE` + `HOMECOMING`. Presets expand into visible ordinary codes and may be customized before confirmation.
  - **Confirmed:** Hero-scoped cheats affect only the activating hero. Session/world cheats affect the team. Any active cheat marks the entire run **Cheat-Assisted**. Co-op participants see the full configuration and both must accept the loss of reward eligibility; session-wide changes always require both participants' confirmation.
  - **Confirmed:** Cheats may be configured in the lobby, briefing, and eligible checkpoints. Solo players may activate them from pause; co-op changes require synchronized consent and apply only after a safe resume boundary. Codes cannot be entered through active combat input. Disabling a cheat later never restores reward eligibility for the current run.
  - **Confirmed:** Cheat-Assisted status remains visible in the HUD, pause screen, readiness state, and results. Results list the canonical codes used and the first activation point. A profile may remember preferred hero-scoped selections, but a new run starts with cheats off unless the player explicitly chooses to reuse them.
  - **Confirmed:** Cheat-Assisted play preserves story and checkpoint progress, stage/campaign completion, difficulty access including Extreme/Ultimate unlocks, cinematics/endings/transcripts, bestiary and special-enemy discoveries, weapon/item discoveries, Guide/gallery entries, relevant Store catalogue reveals, and narrative Story achievements. It awards no score, rank, Credits, Hero Mastery, competitive records, combat/combo/co-op challenge achievements, Academy challenge rewards, or Difficulty achievements.
- **Confirmed:** Relevant cheats are available throughout Story Mode, Chapter Challenge, Character Chronicles, Mission Remix, Curated Missions, and Random Operations. Chapter Challenge and Mission results follow the same Cheat-Assisted reward and record restrictions. Academy permits arbitrary cheats only in Free Practice and Cheat Practice; authored lessons, certification, mastery, and technique-acquisition challenges retain their declared rules.
- **Confirmed:** Cheat modifiers alter only their declared values or state transitions inside the canonical deterministic engine. Boss thresholds, capped carryover, objective and story triggers, surrender logic, anti-infinite budgets, legal boundaries, protected transitions, warnings, network synchronization, and replay rules remain active. `STEADYFEET` preserves safe displacement and bounded hitstun; `IRONGUARD` does not block True Unblockables or remove failed-Counter recovery; `BRIGHTSPIRIT` cannot overlap an active Ultimate or protected boss transition; `STRONGHANDS` respects phase and carryover caps; `TRUSTYTOOLS` does not prevent throws, drops, disarms, transfers, or deterministic recovery; `STEADYRIDE` preserves mounted hit reactions, steering interruption, obstacles, route boundaries, required inputs, and authored safe or boss-transition dismounts, and never turns the mount into a collision weapon.
  - **Confirmed:** Local-Wi-Fi host validation uses canonical cheat identifiers and a versioned cheat-registry hash. Configuration or registry mismatch blocks readiness rather than silently changing a selection. Replays, checkpoints, save snapshots, and My Combos store canonical identifiers rather than localized labels or raw typed text.
  - **Confirmed:** Verification uses a risk-based matrix covering every code alone, all presets, pairwise combinations, explicit conflicts and high-risk interactions, all game modes, keyboard/gamepad operation, three languages, activation boundaries, saves/backups/migration, Academy restrictions, reward gating, difficulty unlocks, bosses, checkpoints, replay hashes, and local-Wi-Fi synchronization. Acceptance permits no progression corruption, peer desynchronization, accidental activation, or mandatory-content softlock.
- **Planned — Module 17: Narrative and Localization.** Elder briefings, story structure, introductions, endings, localization keys, subtitle presentation, and language fallback.
  - **Confirmed:** Every stage follows a **Layered Five-Beat Stage Arc**: Threat, Purpose, Escalation, Confrontation, and Consequence.
  - **Confirmed:** Threat is introduced through a short skippable cinematic or its still-art fallback. Purpose follows in the elder briefing, where the immediate danger, peaceful stakes, objective, relevant warnings, and loadout preparation are presented before readiness.
  - **Confirmed:** Escalation develops primarily through environments, encounter changes, and concise story moments at authored safe boundaries. Dialogue during active danger is brief and non-essential; no required objective or plot information competes with precision combat, defensive cues, or co-op communication.
  - **Confirmed:** Confrontation gives the boss an identity, motivation, and direct relationship to the stage conflict rather than treating the boss as an unexplained final obstacle.
  - **Confirmed:** Consequence shows whom or what the heroes protected, how the village or surrounding world changed, and any future threat established by the ending. Completed-stage consequences may also appear in the Village Command Menu or Collection.
  - **Confirmed:** Previously viewed films and authored story pauses remain skippable or fast-forwardable on replay. Skipping never removes the current objective, essential warning, or later access to a localized recap or transcript.
  - **Confirmed:** Hero-responsive narrative uses a **Canonical Story Spine with Character Modules**. Each stage owns one authoritative threat, objective, boss conflict, and outcome, while selected heroes contribute modular motives, reactions, exchanges, and ending responses.
  - **Confirmed:** Every selected hero receives an authored personal reason to protect the village or affected people and at least one stage-relevant response. In co-op, both heroes receive equal narrative standing; player number, host status, and join order do not make one hero the canonical protagonist.
  - **Confirmed:** Important hero pairs may receive dedicated conversations. When a pair-specific exchange does not exist, the scene composes compatible individual responses in a defined order rather than omitting a hero, repeating a line, or presenting missing content.
  - **Confirmed:** Essential objectives, warnings, boss facts, and outcome facts live in the canonical spine and never depend on an optional character module. Missing or incompatible modules therefore cannot make the stage unclear or unfinishable.
  - **Confirmed:** Core generated cinematics primarily depict the threat, affected people, antagonist, environment, elder, and other team-independent events. Hero-specific identity is normally added through localized dialogue, portraits, text-free character artwork, and transitions, avoiding a separate generated movie for every possible team; exceptional authored hero-specific clips remain possible.
  - **Confirmed:** Every narrative module uses explicit localization keys and declares its eligible heroes, pairs, stage beat, priority, and fallback. Content validation rejects missing required translations and unresolved module fallbacks before release.
  - **Confirmed:** Localized dialogue uses a **Context-Aware Unified Presentation System**. Typography, speaker identity, localization keys, accessibility preferences, and transcript data remain shared, while each narrative context uses the least intrusive suitable presentation.
  - **Confirmed:** Cinematics use safe-area subtitles normally limited to two readable lines with optional speaker labels. Timing respects authored cue boundaries and configured reading support without embedding words into generated footage.
  - **Confirmed:** Elder briefings and other preparation conversations use portraits, speaker names, a full dialogue panel, manual advance, optional auto-advance, and transcript access. Dialogue progression and loadout interaction remain compatible with keyboard, gamepad, and pointer input.
  - **Confirmed:** Gameplay reactions use compact edge-aligned barks that protect the central and lower-middle playfield. Active-danger barks are brief, non-essential, bounded in queue length, and yield to objective warnings, defensive cues, and other higher-priority information.
  - **Confirmed:** Important non-verbal audio may receive separately styled sound captions such as `[gate breaks]`. Spoken dialogue, sound captions, objectives, and combat warnings retain distinct visual roles and cannot rely on color alone.
  - **Confirmed:** Players may configure subtitle size, readable font, background opacity, line width, speaker labels, display duration or reading support, and essential sound-caption presentation. These preferences do not alter combat simulation or score category.
  - **Confirmed:** Skipped or missed narrative dialogue remains available through a localized stage transcript or recap. The transcript preserves speaker order and story-beat context without recording incidental combat noise as dialogue.
  - **Confirmed:** Language ownership uses **Adaptive Profile and Display Language**. Every persistent profile remembers a preferred language, while the rendered display language follows the number of physical screens available.
  - **Confirmed:** Solo uses the active profile's preferred language by default. Same-device co-op uses one clearly displayed shared language selected before readiness; it may be preselected from Player 1's active profile but remains changeable without creating a second simultaneous subtitle track.
  - **Confirmed:** In local-Wi-Fi co-op, each device independently renders menus, briefings, subtitles, captions, and transcripts in its local profile's preferred language. Peers synchronize narrative cue identifiers and authored timing rather than translated sentence text or rendered text length.
  - **Confirmed:** Language changes never alter simulation or score category. A currently open briefing or menu rerenders immediately where safe, while an active cinematic changes subtitle language at the next cue boundary to avoid mixing languages inside one subtitle.
  - **Confirmed:** Released content must pass completeness, font-coverage, line-wrapping, overflow, and timing checks for English, Simplified Chinese, and German. Generated images and video contain no baked-in language-dependent words.
  - **Confirmed:** If localized data is unexpectedly missing or damaged at runtime, the affected entry falls back to English rather than displaying blank text, and a diagnostic is recorded. Release validation still treats missing required translations as an error rather than relying on fallback as normal presentation.
  - **Confirmed direction:** Stage preparation follows `Stage selected → skippable intro cinematic → elder briefing and loadout → player readiness → gameplay`.
  - **Confirmed direction:** The cinematic establishes the immediate threat, while the elder briefing uses text-free artwork plus localized dialogue to explain objectives, relevant warnings, and the reason for fighting. Players may review and change their hero loadout before confirming readiness.
  - **Confirmed direction:** Same-device co-op provides distinct player loadout panels. In local-Wi-Fi co-op, each player may skip the cinematic locally and enter briefing preparation independently; gameplay begins only after both players confirm readiness.
  - **Confirmed direction:** If a cinematic is unavailable, unsupported, or intentionally disabled, the same narrative and preparation flow uses still artwork plus localized text without removing the briefing or loadout step.
  - **Confirmed direction:** Google Flow with Veo is the preferred generation tool for Stage introductions and other story cinematics.
  - **Confirmed direction:** Generated cinematic footage contains no embedded words. English, Simplified Chinese, and German subtitles remain separate game-interface layers driven by localization keys.
  - **Confirmed direction:** Cinematic dialogue is subtitle-first. Spoken dialogue is deferred until a separate localized-voice strategy is approved; music, ambience, and synchronized non-verbal sounds may accompany the footage.
  - **Confirmed direction:** The elder's personal photo may be supplied as an ordinary visual reference or ingredient with the creator's consent. The production pipeline must not depend on Google Flow's Avatar feature, which may be unavailable by region.
  - **Confirmed direction:** Story cinematics are assembled from multiple short shots rather than relying on one long generation. Every sequence must be skippable, captioned, and able to fall back to still artwork plus localized text.
- **Planned — Module 18: Cinematics, Art, Animation, Music, and SFX Pipeline.** Visual style, elder-photo transformation, cinematic generation and editing, sprites, animation requirements, effects, music, sound, browser delivery, and asset manifests.
  - **Confirmed:** Interactive characters use an **Anchored Hybrid Sprite Pipeline**. Each character begins with a model sheet and approved production seed frame defining silhouette, palette, proportions, key features, and outfit details before additional combat animation is produced.
  - **Confirmed:** Candidate action animations are generated or drawn as complete strips with an exact frame count and layout rather than as unrelated individual images. The strip preserves character identity, facing, silhouette family, palette family, proportions, outfit, transparent background, and production-asset composition.
  - **Confirmed:** Every strip is normalized with one shared scale and bottom-center anchor. When continuity requires it, frame one is locked back to the shipped seed pose so idle-to-action and action-to-action transitions begin from an exact known frame.
  - **Confirmed:** Human cleanup and review correct hands, faces, clothing, weapons, transparency, motion arcs, frame-to-frame drift, and silhouette readability. A generated strip is a candidate asset, not an automatically accepted production animation.
  - **Confirmed:** Startup, active, recovery, cancel windows, movement, hitboxes, hurtboxes, armor, resource effects, and other combat behavior remain authored deterministic data independent of sprite pixels. Generated artwork never determines gameplay timing or collision automatically.
  - **Confirmed:** Every animation receives an in-engine preview at gameplay scale and validation for identity stability, anchor stability, silhouette clarity, transition continuity, browser memory, and performance before its asset index is updated.
  - **Confirmed:** Detachable weapons and effects remain separate layers where practical to avoid multiplying body-animation strips. Rigging remains available for subtle idle motion, portraits, backgrounds, and selected transitions, but does not replace authored combat poses where extreme perspective, ground states, throws, or impact require unique frames.
  - **Confirmed:** Combat presentation uses **Layered Scalable Spectacle**. Every successful contact retains a mandatory readable impact core, while cosmetic sparks, particles, trails, flashes, hit-stop emphasis, and camera motion scale according to the authored importance of the move.
  - **Confirmed:** Ordinary attacks remain restrained; heavy attacks, weapons, and launchers may use moderate trails, debris, and camera impulse; Exact Blocks, Counters, and Combo Breakers receive strong distinct signatures; Ultimates, co-op finishers, and boss transitions receive the largest presentation that does not conceal incoming hazards, partners, or defensive timing.
  - **Confirmed:** A bounded per-screen effect budget limits or merges overlapping cosmetic effects during crowded encounters. Essential hit confirmation, attack telegraphs, objective warnings, defensive cues, and deterministic timing are never removed by the budget.
  - **Confirmed:** Camera shake, flashes, particle density, and similar cosmetic layers are independently adjustable. Reduced-motion and adaptive-quality settings may soften or remove cosmetic layers but preserve essential timing and state information through non-color-only cues.
  - **Confirmed:** Visual and audio effects are presentation-only consumers of combat events. They never alter collision, frame data, random outcomes, scoring, or local-Wi-Fi synchronization.
  - **Confirmed direction:** Google Flow with Veo is the preferred tool for cinematic prototypes and candidate final video clips, including intro ambience and synchronized cinematic sound effects.
  - **Confirmed direction:** Google Flow Music with Lyria is the preferred tool for soundtrack prototypes and candidate final music. Generated tracks require human review and post-production for structure, seamless looping, transitions, loudness, and browser-ready exports.
  - **Confirmed:** Stage music uses an **Authored Adaptive Score**. Each stage begins with one coherent musical family that is human-edited into compatible loopable sections, transition stingers, and, where useful, a small number of optional intensity stems rather than unrelated runtime-selected songs.
  - **Confirmed:** Authored states may include arrival or exploration, ordinary combat, elevated danger, boss combat, victory, and story moments. State changes occur at declared musical boundaries or through authored transition cues so gameplay events do not cause arbitrary hard cuts.
  - **Confirmed:** The runtime selects declared music states and compatible layers; it does not generate, improvise, or freely remix music during play. Music decisions remain presentation-only and do not affect deterministic combat or scoring.
  - **Confirmed:** Lower-quality or constrained devices may use browser-ready pre-mixed state tracks instead of simultaneous stems. The fallback preserves the same musical identity and state meanings while reducing decoding, memory, and mixing cost.
  - **Confirmed:** Google Flow Music with Lyria output remains candidate source material. Final adoption requires human review and editing for thematic consistency, compatible key and tempo, clean loop points, transitions, loudness, licensed-use records, and compressed cross-browser delivery.
  - **Confirmed:** The approved campaign music families and cue suites for Stages 1–8 and both Random Operations objective families are defined in `docs/production/campaign_music_direction.md`. Stage 2's existing seven-cue **Prosperity Under Siege** suite remains unchanged; the new Stage 1 suite adds presentation content without changing locked gameplay or story.
  - **Confirmed:** Random Operations uses separate **Five Waves, One Stand** and **Until Help Arrives** families for Defeat and Safeguard. Seeds select declared states and spoiler-safe validated arena overlays rather than arbitrary songs, and content from uncompleted stages remains unavailable.
  - **Confirmed direction:** Gameplay sound effects use a separate dedicated SFX or foley pipeline. Mixed audio extracted from generated video is not the default source for low-latency combat, weapon, item, or interface sounds.
  - **Confirmed:** Crowded combat uses a **Priority-Aware Combat Mix** with authored category priorities and simultaneous-voice limits rather than playing every triggered sound at equal weight.
  - **Confirmed:** Player-action confirmation, Exact Block, Counter, Combo Breaker, boss telegraphs, objective warnings, and other essential defensive or danger information receive the highest protection. Important contacts and weapons follow; redundant crowd impacts, repeated voices, debris, and ambience may merge, attenuate, or yield.
  - **Confirmed:** Voice selection considers authored importance, listener distance, player ownership, recency, and duplication. Repeated barks and near-identical impacts are rate-limited so crowds do not create an unintelligible chorus or conceal a new critical cue.
  - **Confirmed:** Exceptional cues may apply brief bounded ducking to music and ambience, but do not silence the battle. The mixer protects headroom and avoids clipping while preserving a physical, energetic presentation.
  - **Confirmed:** Music, sound effects, spoken dialogue if later added, interface sounds, and ambience have independent volume controls. Essential non-verbal information has optional localized sound captions and cannot rely on audio alone.
  - **Confirmed:** Performance settings may reduce cosmetic simultaneous voices and variation pools without removing essential warnings or confirmation cues. Audio playback remains presentation-only and never changes combat resolution or synchronization.
  - **Confirmed direction:** Generated media keeps its provenance markers, including SynthID where present. The asset manifest records the tool, model, prompt, date, references, source files, edits, and export settings for every candidate asset.
  - **Confirmed:** Assets use a **Versioned Modular Asset Graph**. Gameplay, narrative, localization, and presentation data reference stable logical asset identifiers rather than physical filenames.
  - **Confirmed:** Stage, hero, weapon, interface, localization, cinematic, music, and sound packages own focused manifests that compose into one validated release index instead of requiring every feature to edit one monolithic manual registry.
  - **Confirmed:** Manifest entries record source tool and model, prompt and creation date where applicable, source references, retained provenance markers, human edit history, usage-rights records, locale and quality variants, fallbacks, dependencies, delivery-bundle membership, and compatibility versions.
  - **Confirmed:** Final browser-ready files receive content hashes for integrity checks, reliable caching, and reproducible releases. The immutable release index records the exact resolved content set shipped by a game version.
  - **Confirmed:** Build validation rejects broken logical references, missing required translations or fallbacks, unsupported formats, dependency cycles, incompatible versions, and incomplete provenance or rights fields before release.
  - **Confirmed:** Replacing or regenerating a physical asset creates a new resolved asset version without forcing gameplay definitions to change. Save and replay compatibility checks use logical content and compatibility versions rather than incidental filenames.
  - **Confirmed direction:** Final adoption requires a Stage 1 media pilot that validates character and environment consistency, music quality and loopability, clean audio separation, browser performance and file size, localization compatibility, and acceptable usage-rights risk.
  - **Confirmed direction:** Intro video begins only after a player interaction so browser audio can start reliably. It must support skip, pause, captions, volume control, preloading, and compressed cross-browser video and audio fallbacks.
  - **Confirmed:** Browser delivery uses **Staged Predictive Bundles** rather than preloading the entire game or fetching required assets only when they are first used. The menu shell starts quickly, while the selected heroes, current stage essentials, common combat presentation, and first encounter are guaranteed before gameplay begins.
  - **Confirmed:** Briefings, safe transitions, and other known pauses prefetch the next likely encounter, weapon, enemy, boss, music, and cinematic bundles. Explicit readiness gates prevent gameplay from entering a section whose required combat assets are unavailable.
  - **Confirmed:** Optional high-resolution media and cosmetic variants load after essential gameplay assets. Under a declared memory budget, inactive optional bundles may be released while reusable core assets remain cached.
  - **Confirmed:** Cinematics stream separately where appropriate and retain the approved still-artwork and localized-text fallback. Missing optional media activates a defined presentation fallback instead of blocking deterministic combat.
  - **Confirmed:** Weak or interrupted connections may delay a readiness gate or select an approved quality fallback but never change simulation behavior. Local-Wi-Fi peers synchronize gameplay readiness and content compatibility, not identical byte-level loading progress.
  - **Confirmed:** Every delivery bundle declares its required dependencies, approximate size, quality variants, fallback assets, integrity information, and lifecycle policy in the asset manifest.
- **Planned — Module 19: Stage 1 Content Design.** Story, environment, encounter sequence, enemies, containers, food, weapons, boss, pacing, and ending.
  - **Confirmed:** Stage 1 uses **The Broken Water Pact**. An armed faction seizes the shared river gate, diverts the village's water, and coerces local workers, placing both the captives and the village's survival in immediate danger.
  - **Confirmed:** The elder first attempts negotiation and analyzes the occupation before asking the two heroes to intervene. Their stated objective is to free the workers, restore the water, and prevent further harm—not to seize territory, take revenge, or destroy the opposing faction.
  - **Confirmed:** Stage 1 progresses through dry village fields, an occupied bridge, a working mill and canal route, and the seized river gate. These locations support visible environmental storytelling, water and machinery hazards, containers, food, improvised weapons, civilian rescues, and escalating combat lessons.
  - **Confirmed:** The occupying force contains committed aggressors alongside coerced workers or desperate recruits, allowing localized dialogue and enemy behavior to distinguish levels of responsibility. Surrendering or fleeing characters are not treated as active combat targets.
  - **Confirmed:** The gatehouse boss may be defeated, disarmed, and captured or driven to surrender without an execution. Restoring the river provides the immediate visual and emotional victory.
  - **Confirmed:** Evidence found at the gate shows that an unknown outside sponsor financed or equipped the occupation, providing a larger-story hook without withholding Stage 1's complete local resolution.
  - **Confirmed:** Stage 1 uses an **Escalating Encounter Arc** with authored breathing spaces. Focused early fights introduce one concept at a time, short rescue, story, or supply beats allow recovery and experimentation, and later encounters combine learned threats before the boss tests the complete Stage 1 skill set.
  - **Confirmed:** The eight major beats are: dry-fields fundamentals; occupied-bridge formation test; worker rescue and first recovery cache; working-mill ground, weapon, and machinery encounter; short investigation and preparation breath; canal-route mixed-enemy escalation; gate-courtyard elite exam; and a multi-phase gate commander.
  - **Confirmed:** The dry fields teach movement, basic offense, guard, and simple enemy behavior. The bridge introduces lane control, throws, formation pressure, and the first strong co-op opportunity without immediately combining every advanced mechanic.
  - **Confirmed:** The mill and canal progressively introduce ground routes, weapons, environmental danger, aerial pressure, group attacks, and mixed tactical roles. Recovery caches, civilians, investigation clues, and short traversal provide pacing relief without becoming unrelated minigames.
  - **Confirmed:** Stage 1's environmental content is deliberately representative rather than exhaustive: one authored impact surface, one multi-state breakable producing one usable fragment, one active mill or canal hazard, one cooperative redirect opportunity, and one Resolve-gated boss interaction. These elements receive a safe first-use introduction, one Academy exercise, and Combat Lab diagnostics.
  - **Confirmed:** Stage 1 does not require horses, motorbikes, wagons, revolvers, machine guns, grenades, dedicated mounted enemies, or armed-enemy firearm encounters. Those systems remain later-stage expansion content even when their future schemas are considered during architecture work.
  - **Confirmed:** The gate courtyard recombines the stage's learned enemy roles as an elite readiness test. The gate commander then uses authored standing, aerial, ground, weapon, defense, phase-transition, and anti-infinite behavior rather than introducing an unrelated ruleset.
  - **Confirmed:** Exact wave composition, spawn timing, resource placement, enemy variants, and optional challenge formations remain difficulty-driven content data. Higher difficulties may add enemies and formations while preserving the same narrative route and essential tutorial readability.
  - **Confirmed:** After the boss, the heroes restore the river, confirm civilian safety, and discover the outside sponsor's evidence. The stage therefore resolves its immediate conflict before presenting the hook for the next stage.
- **Planned — Module 20: Technical Architecture.** Browser framework, combat runtime, data schemas, save system, localization, networking, asset loading, and automated testing.
  - **Confirmed:** The browser game uses a **Layered Phaser Architecture** built with TypeScript and Vite. The deterministic simulation remains framework-independent; Phaser is the 2D renderer and scene coordinator rather than the owner of gameplay truth.
  - **Confirmed:** A fixed-step TypeScript core owns canonical entities, movement, combat, AI, items, weapons, encounters, resources, scoring, random seeds, progression-relevant results, snapshots, and other rollback-safe state. It contains no Phaser display objects, tweens, cameras, audio nodes, DOM elements, or device APIs.
  - **Confirmed:** Canonical schemas support versioned environmental object state, break integrity, interactive fragments, hazard actions, environmental combo quotas, and persistence policies. Later schema extensions may add mount profiles, Stability, vehicle roles, advanced-weapon targeting, ammunition, resupply state, and physical ownership without moving those rules into Phaser scenes.
  - **Confirmed:** Phaser scenes remain thin and focus on boot, staged asset loading, playfield orchestration, sprite and animation presentation, cameras, parallax, particles, visual effects, and world-audio presentation. Renderer-facing objects are disposable projections of simulation state.
  - **Confirmed:** Text-heavy and accessibility-sensitive surfaces use a DOM interface layer over or beside the Phaser canvas. This includes the game shell, briefings, loadouts, HUD, pause, settings, subtitles, Academy, store, records, and localized dialogue. The architecture does not require React or Next.js for these surfaces.
  - **Confirmed:** Keyboard, gamepad, save storage, portable profile files, Web Audio, browser capability checks, asset manifests, visibility and focus handling, and WebRTC are isolated behind explicit platform adapters. Physical inputs are converted into the approved abstract action format before reaching the simulation.
  - **Confirmed:** One explicit bridge carries frame-stamped actions into the core and exposes serializable snapshots plus stable presentation events to Phaser and the DOM. Presentation events use stable identities so rollback and resimulation cannot duplicate audiovisual feedback.
  - **Confirmed:** Solo, same-device co-op, local-Wi-Fi host and client, replays, Combat Lab scenarios, and headless automated tests execute the same simulation modules and authored content schemas rather than simplified parallel implementations.
  - **Confirmed:** Vite supplies the development server, TypeScript-oriented client build, asset processing, code splitting, and optimized static production output. Full type checking, linting, unit tests, deterministic replay tests, schema validation, content validation, and production builds run as explicit separate quality gates.
  - **Confirmed:** The game client can be deployed as static browser assets. The minimal ephemeral pairing/signaling service remains a separate deployment boundary and cannot own combat state, saves, progression, or authoritative gameplay simulation.
  - **Confirmed:** A later public website or broader account-backed online platform may use Next.js alongside the Vite game in a shared repository, but adopting such a web platform does not move deterministic combat into React or require the game client to become a Next.js application.
- **Planned — Module 21: Stage 1 Vertical Slice.** Integration and validation of the complete first stage from introduction through boss and ending.
  - **Confirmed:** The milestone uses a **Representative Vertical Slice** boundary. It must deliver the complete Stage 1 journey from skippable intro movie and briefing through the four environment beats, boss, ending, results, and saved progression rather than accepting an isolated combat arena as sufficient evidence.
  - **Confirmed:** Acceptance proves all three game modes; both founding heroes without duplicate selection; both Stage 1 weapons as in-stage acquisitions; the approved Stage 1 enemy roster and boss; the confirmed combat, defense, co-op, weapon, anti-infinite, difficulty, score, Credit, profile, save, loadout, and remapping foundations; one representative Academy teaching route; English, Simplified Chinese, and German narrative presentation; the generated-media integration pilot; data-driven content loading; supported-browser capability and performance checks; and freedom from progression-blocking defects.
  - **Confirmed:** Acceptance also proves the Representative Environmental Slice, including deterministic damage and quota behavior, break-state persistence, one interactive debris profile, one active hazard response, one co-op redirect, one boss Resolve interaction, checkpoint restoration, replay consistency, local-Wi-Fi synchronization, accessibility, and adaptive-presentation protection for functional cues.
  - **Confirmed:** Optional placeholder mount or advanced-weapon compatibility experiments do not become Stage 1 acceptance gates and do not authorize production media or encounters for those later-stage systems.
  - **Confirmed:** The slice uses a deliberately bounded content catalogue and representative difficulty coverage. Final numerical balance, every store item, every achievement, every purchased move, Ultimate difficulty completion, complete final media polish, and later-stage content are production work rather than vertical-slice acceptance requirements.
- **Status:** The high-level game, combat, content, media-pipeline, and browser-architecture refinement pass is complete. Remaining provisional numbers and unresolved implementation constants are Combat Lab tuning, validation, production-content, and implementation-planning work rather than undecided feature models.
- **Confirmed:** Phase 3 requirements may be brought forward when they affect current combat data, such as Academy move metadata or localization keys.

## 12. Specification readiness and remaining production work

- **Confirmed:** Stage 7, **The Hidden Ledger**, uses a **Six-Link Ledger Exposure Route**: Yunzhou Council Forward Office, Shell Consultancy Office, Freight Service Corridor, Regional Freight Exchange, Meridian Forum Public Wing and Forum Branch Archive. It targets 48–56 minutes first clear, 33–41 replay, nine mandatory encounters plus the Compact Erasure Chief, two forward-reconnecting optional branches and three safe evidence-review transitions. Tomas's annex begins authentication; Damon acquisition, Rhea operations and Han strategy records are recovered and matched in distinct spaces. Witnesses/workers/Forum civilians are protected, a short electric cargo-vehicle link avoids a full driving mode, and missed interceptions affect condition/rank rather than canonical recovery. The chief is captured alive and the route remains smaller than Stage 8.
- **Confirmed:** Stage 7 opens with **Converging Testimony**, a 25–35-second skippable spoiler-safe sequence connecting record removal, freight diversion and Tomas's voluntary unarmed arrival with the Stage 6 annex. He relinquishes it and enters lawful protective custody without pardon or reconciliation; it proves urgency but not identity. Kael/Wayfarer Watch independently answer witness requests, cooperating without joining the patrol. Priorities are people, original evidence, live capture and proportionate force. Selectable Story heroes are Mountain Guardian, River Scout, Wind Sentinel, Fire Warden and guest Kael; solo selects one/co-op two unique heroes. Unselected Kael remains in support scenes, while Soren/Chen Yue are non-playable Story allies with their existing Mission Mode availability.
- **Confirmed:** Stage 7 uses an **Authenticated Three-Ledger Chain**: Damon Acquisition Ledger at the consultancy, Rhea Operations Register at the freight exchange and Han Strategy Memorandum at the Forum Archive. Each progresses Unlocated→Identified→Under Erasure→Stabilized→Secured→Authenticated with Preserved/Reconstructed/Essential Core integrity. Warned erasure lowers one step; attempted loss below Essential Core restores a checkpoint. Accessible safe-transition matching uses IDs/timestamps/signatures/routing/custody without timed reading or guessing. All three are indispensable: together they reveal the Meridian Compact, steering circle and two-of-three authority and establish Stage 8 grounds. Earlier clues corroborate only. Integrity affects performance/Museum presentation, never reveal or access.
- **Confirmed:** Stage 7 uses a **Protected Transfer System** for mandatory consultancy, freight and Forum civilian groups: Sheltered→Route Authorized→Protected Transfer→Council Safe, with heroes clearing/accessing/defending routes and Wayfarer/Council protected handoff rather than follower escort. Conditions Protected/Disrupted/Emergency Extracted affect presentation/performance only; all people survive with no Health/grab/shield/environment state, and Witness Grabbers target route controls. Solo allows one total evidence/civilian pressure, co-op two staggered separate targets. Safe groups lock permanently. Optional witnesses remain supplemental.
- **Confirmed:** Stage 7 Shell Consultancy Office has mandatory encounters **Open the Safe Route** and **The Acquisition Ledger**. The first clears reception/corridor/safety lobby, manually releases two doors, protects the consultancy transfer and introduces Suppressor/Grabber/Escort under protected-workplace rules. The second locates Damon's physical ledger, stabilizes its signed token/mirror, defends warned erasure and secures Council custody. Zero/one/two completed actions produce Preserved/Reconstructed/Essential Core; another restores checkpoint. Shutters have manual release, tagged cleanup partitions/consoles are legal, lawful cabinets/records are protected, and the safe review matches Tomas's fragment without revealing the organization.
- **Confirmed:** Stage 7 mandatory **Intercept the Cleanup Convoy** is a 2–3-minute evacuated Freight Service Corridor using existing mounts: one finite-Stability electric cargo bike solo/per hero co-op, safe/voluntary dismount and equivalent on-foot path. Break the Screen, Clear the Barrier and Stop the Credential Transfer Pressure Gates yield no setup at 3, one minor setup at 2, or reinforced shutters plus one ordinary slot within cap at 0–1. The courier always reaches the perimeter; results affect opening/score/commentary only. Bikes cannot ram civilians/explode/carry evidence/be weaponized, shared-camera/regroup prevents softlock, and vehicle state ends at the exchange.
- **Confirmed:** Stage 7 Freight Exchange has mandatory **Hold the Dispatch Floor** and **Orders in Transit**. The first clears three lanes, restores/defends freight-worker transfer and introduces a local warned Technology Disabler unable to affect people/evidence/checkpoints/sole routes/permanent state; corridor results adjust only barrier/shutter/one capped ordinary slot. The second secures Rhea's signed command terminal, dispatch seals and route-history mirror under the shared three-step integrity rule. Deterministic lifts/carts provide safe cover/tagged redirects without free driving or protected harm. Safe review matches Damon IDs and proves centralized command without completing the steering-circle reveal.
- **Confirmed:** Stage 7 Forum Public Wing has mandatory **Separate the Public Wing** and **The Strategy Index**. The first uses Council non-color/behavior identification, three evacuation routes and false-redirection prevention; only marked cleanup personnel are enemies, while ordinary Forum security withdraws/surrenders/assists and the mandatory Forum group enters Protected Transfer. The second protects the public catalogue, recovers non-graded Han locator/access metadata, restores the archive lift and prevents handshake corruption. A completed action causes a longer manual route/time debit, never loss. Public property/access is protected, cleanup structures are legal, manual release always exists, and Compact naming waits for ledger authentication.
- **Confirmed:** Stage 7 Archive has mandatory **The Hidden Ledger** and **Hold the Three Keys**. The first secures Han's signed policy folio, two-of-three approval key and meeting mirror under the shared integrity rule. The second defensively authenticates three custody-held ledgers across three nodes; hostile completion only resets one node progress/adds an efficiency incident and cannot lower secured integrity. Completion reveals Meridian Compact, Han/Rhea/Damon roles, approval system and Stage 8 grounds. The Erasure Chief enters immediately with no intervening wave. Original evidence/history is protected; tagged cleanup equipment is legal and evidence never becomes a weapon.
- **Confirmed:** Stage 7 standardizes one shared **Council Field Support Robot** at marked points with one allocation per supported encounter: Mobile Cover, Objective Interference that only pauses a hostile channel, or Reserve Delay without deleting budget. One effect runs; visible activation permits one cooldown retry after first disruption, while second disruption or success consumes. Disablers suppress temporarily only; difficulty cannot weaken it and checkpoints store full state. It cannot damage/combo, handle evidence, complete/open objectives, revive, capture, auto-protect or collect. Selected Office/Freight/Public Wing/Archive/boss uses all have unassisted solutions.
- **Confirmed:** Stage 7 temporary equipment is Expandable Control Baton, finite single-load Compact Sidearm, one-magazine Compact Automatic and one-carried fictional Concussion Device with no protected harm. Stage-only authorized/disarmed pickups cannot be pre-equipped/stored/purchased/exported; exact ammo/ownership/disposal persists, with no reload/refill and finite warned enemy fire. Authored cleanup-owned breakables use Intact→Damaged→Broken and safe fragments. Three Protected/Disturbed/Damaged ledgers cover consultancy/public property, freight safety and Forum/archive preservation, counting only avoidable player incidents. People/evidence/critical history/controls/routes are immune; impacts obey shared anti-loop/elite/breaker rules.
- **Confirmed:** Stage 7 core enemies are Archive Suppressor, route/control-targeting Witness Grabber, finite-Guard Shield Escort and local Technology Disabler. Extreme cumulatively adds Erasure Specialist accelerating one eligible warned channel without passing Essential Core; Ultimate retains it and adds an interruptible finite-buff Counter-Intelligence Captain without false evidence/warnings/budget. Active caps reuse Stage 6: solo/co-op Easy 4/6, Medium 5/7, Hard–Ultimate 6/8. Six formations obey solo one total objective/one aim/one specialist and co-op two staggered separate targets; specialists never overlap, reserves cannot act, boss has no adds, and difficulty changes coordination rather than waves/Health.
- **Confirmed:** Stage 7 boss is **Silas Kern — The Erasure Chief / 西拉斯·克恩——抹除主管 / Silas Kern – Leiter der Spurenvernichtung**, a 44-year-old Rhea cleanup commander, not a steering leader. Controlled Access uses baton/finite projection shield; Suppression Geometry uses three fixed warned emitters with solo one/co-op two staggered safe lanes and visible robot suppression; one-shot Last Redaction has three steps affecting only supplemental Archive Preservation, never the ledger chain. Interruption or emergency isolation enters no-reset Closed File with no retry/resources/adds. Zero Resolve yields live custody and limited Rhea confirmation.
- **Confirmed:** Stage 7 uses Tomas's **Two-Step Voluntary Surrender**. Opening handover gives original annex, abandonment copy, routing fragment and authentication metadata under documented rights/search/medical/custody, with no immunity/pardon/reduction/contact/asset protection; it corroborates only. After independent proof he accepts specified adult responsibility, ends collective revenge, submits Compact-linked claims to restitution and cooperates without demanding forgiveness. Kael/Xu Lian retain contact/forgiveness agency; Kael's alliance is independent. Tomas remains a fixed noncombatant custody outcome, evidence cannot be lost, and he learns Compact identity only after the heroes establish it.
- **Confirmed:** Stage 7 optional **Voices Behind the Door / 门后证言 / Stimmen hinter der Tür** protects supplemental consultancy witnesses and adds recollections/art/commentary; **Routes of the Forgotten / 被遗忘者的路线 / Wege der Vergessenen** secures three indestructible victim-route folios and restitution context. Neither contains mandatory evidence or advantage. Base clear grants **Forum Public History Exhibit**; both create **Forum Accountability Gallery** plus **Truth Has Many Witnesses / 真相有众多见证 / Die Wahrheit hat viele Zeugen**.
